1	# Notes
2	
3	See [design.md](design.md)
4	
5	
6	## jj tips
7	
8	For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
9	
10	```
11	## Chores format
12	
13	Filename: "chores-XX.md"
14	example: chores-01.md
15	
16	Format of section labels: "## <short description> (X.Y.Z)"
17	example: "## Topic format description (0.1.0)"
18	
19	Example chore file:
20	```
21	# Chores-01.md
22	 
23	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
24	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
25	how and why of a particular chore.
26	
27	## Do something (1.3.1)
28	
29	Describe something
30	```
31	
32	## Versioning during development
33	
34	This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
35	git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
36	we go.
37	
38	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
39	
40	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
41	  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
42	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
43	  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
44	
45	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
46	
47	For multi-step:
48	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
49	2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
50	3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
51	
52	The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
53	prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
54	commits were exploratory vs final.
55	
56	## Todo format
57	
58	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
59	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
60	references.
61	
62	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
63	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
64	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
65	
66	Examples:
67	
68	# Todo
69	
70	- Add new feature X [details](chores-01.md#feature-x)
71	- Fix bug Y [1]
72	
73	# Done
74	
75	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
76	
77	[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
78	
```

1	# actor-x1
2	
3	## Overview
4	
5	Actor-x1 is a system that uses Communicating
6	Sequential Process (CSP) invented by Tony Hoare
7	In 1978 and is commonly termed the actor model.
8	
9	 - There will be a management system (MS) that:
10	
11	   - Provides the capability to interact with
12	     the runtime providing control and to capture
13	     logs and assists in developing the app
14	   - Creates an hello world scaffolding
15	   - provides discovery capabilities of other
16	     other actors.
17	
18	 - A runtime that drives the system and provides
19	   resources and services for the system
20	   - interfaces between the manager and the application
21	 
22	 - One or more actors running on one or more
23	   threads. Each actor adheres to these
24	   invariants:
25	   - Has on basic entry point; handler()
26	   - The handler() handles one message at a time and
27	     must run to completion as quickly as possible.
28	   - The handler() never ever blocks
29	   - If a handler() has tasks that take to much time
30	     a technique that can be used is to spawn
31	     threads to do work. That thread would then use
32	     messages to communicate with its parent and
33	     other actors as it does its work.
34	
35	## Future capabilities
36	   - I would like Actors to be implemented as an HSM.
37	     This potentially allows an Actor to implement
38	     sophisticated algorithms and prove correct operation.
39	
40	## Stage1 runtime
41	
42	Design and implement the simplest runtime possible
43	
44	### Message
45	
46	struct Message {}
47	
48	### Actor
49	
50	Actor implements an Actor trait
51	
52	trait Actor {
53	  // Handles a message 
54	  fn handle_message(msg: Message)
55	
56	  // other methods..
57	}
58	
59	### Goal1
60	
61	- Run two actors on one thread.
62	- A single message which is an empty struct
63	- Message ping pongs between the two actors
64	  for duration in seconds specified as a f64
65	  in seconds from the command line
66	
67	## Goal2
68	
69	- Same as Goal1 but each actor on a separate thread.
70	
71	## Stage2 runtime
72	
73	We will design and implement the slightly more capable runtime
74	
75	It supports 3 actors A, B and C
76	
77	### Message
78	
79	struct Message {
80	  src_id: u32,
81	  dst_id: u32,
82	  send_count: u64,
83	}
84	
85	### Actor
86	
87	Actor implements an Actor trait
88	
89	trait Actor {
90	  // Create a new actor
91	  fn new(id: u32, name: &str) -> Self;
92	
93	  // Return the actors name
94	  fn name(&Self) -> &str;
95	
96	  // Handle a message
97	  fn handle_message(msg: mut Message);
98	
99	  // other methods..
100	}
101	
102	### Goal1
103	
104	- Run 3 actors on one thread.
105	- Runtime "sends" 5 messages each to a random destination actor
106	- Each actor forwards the message they receive to a random
107	  dst_id that they select.
108	
109	### Goal2
110	- Same as Goal1 but each actor on a separate thread.
111	
```

1	# Chores-01
2	
3	Discussions and notes on various chores in github compatible markdown.
4	There is also a [todo.md](todo.md) file and it tracks tasks and in
5	general there should be a chore section for each task with the why
6	and how this task will be completed.
7	
8	See [Chores format](README.md#chores-format)
9	
10	## Stage 1 runtime — plan marker (0.1.0-0)
11	
12	Begins the Stage 1 implementation described in [design.md](design.md).
13	Multi-step ladder (see [Versioning during development](../CLAUDE.md#versioning)):
14	
15	- `0.1.0-0` — bootstrap Cargo crate + this plan marker (no behavior).
16	- `0.1.0-1` — Goal1: two actors, single thread, ping-pong an empty
17	  `Message` for a caller-supplied duration in seconds. Reports
18	  total messages handled.
19	- `0.1.0-2` — vendor `tprobe2` (and its transitive deps: `band_table`,
20	  `ticks`, `fmt_commas*`) from `../iiac-perf/src` into `src/perf/`.
21	  Wrap per-dispatch `handle_message` calls with `probe.start`/`probe.end`
22	  using `site_id = actor_id`; print a band-table report at shutdown
23	  (ns by default, `-t/--ticks` for raw ticks). The bot thinks promoting
24	  this to a shared crate is worthwhile once Stage 2 is underway, but
25	  copying in now avoids a cross-repo dependency for the PoC.
26	- `0.1.0-3` — Goal2: same workload as Goal1 but each actor on its own
27	  thread, communicating via `std::sync::mpsc` channels. Shutdown by
28	  dropping senders. Probe instrumented the same way (per-thread
29	  `TProbe2`, report on join).
30	- `0.1.0` — final: drop the `-N` suffix, update `notes/todo.md` and
31	  `notes/README.md`.
32	
33	### Design decisions recorded here
34	
35	- **Actor trait signature** deviates from the design sketch by adding
36	  `&mut self` and a `&mut dyn Context` parameter, where `Context::send(dst_id, msg)`
37	  is the only way for a handler to emit messages. Required because
38	  the empty `Message {}` carries no reply-to information and the
39	  sketched signature `fn handle_message(msg: Message)` has nowhere
40	  to thread outbound sends through.
41	- **Two `Context` implementations**: the single-threaded runtime's
42	  context writes to a `VecDeque`; the multi-threaded runtime's
43	  context holds `Sender<Message>` per destination. Actors see
44	  only `&mut dyn Context` and are agnostic to which runtime is
45	  driving them.
46	- **x86_64-only for now**: `tprobe2`'s tick counter uses `rdtsc`
47	  and `iiac-perf/src/ticks.rs` emits a `compile_error!` on other
48	  arches. Development target is x86_64 so this is acceptable; the
49	  restriction is documented at the vendored module.
50	
51	## Goal1: single-thread ping-pong runtime (0.1.0-1)
52	
53	Implements Goal1 of Stage 1: two actors, one thread, ping-pong an
54	empty [`Message`] for a caller-supplied duration in seconds, then
55	report the message count and throughput.
56	
57	- `src/lib.rs`: `Message` unit struct, `Actor` trait
58	  (`handle_message(&mut self, &mut dyn Context, Message)`),
59	  `Context` trait (`send(dst_id: u32, msg: Message)`), and
60	  `pub mod runtime`.
61	- `src/runtime.rs`: `SingleThreadRuntime` owns a
62	  `Vec<Box<dyn Actor>>` and a `VecDeque<(u32, Message)>`; `run_for`
63	  pops a message, field-split-borrows `actors` and `queue`,
64	  constructs a private `SingleCtx` wrapping the queue, dispatches,
65	  counts. Terminates on deadline or queue drain. Unit tests cover
66	  seed drain, bounded ping-pong count (11 = seed + 5·2), and
67	  sequential id assignment.
68	- `src/bin/goal1.rs`: CLI takes one positional `<duration_secs>`
69	  f64; constructs two `PingPongActor`s (each replies once per
70	  received message to its peer), seeds one message into actor 0,
71	  runs, prints `goal1: <count> messages in <secs>s (<M msg/s>)`.
72	
73	Smoke run on this box: 0.5 s → ~19.3 M messages → ~38.7 M msg/s.
74	
75	### Deviation from the design sketch
76	
77	`struct Message {}` in `design.md` is realised as a unit struct
78	`pub struct Message;` — equivalent externally, instantiates as
79	`Message` rather than `Message {}`. Matches clippy's preference for
80	empty types; trivial to convert back if literal match matters.
81	
82	## Vendored perf probe + Goal1 instrumentation (0.1.0-2)
83	
84	Copies `tprobe2` and its transitive dependencies into the crate and
85	wraps every `handle_message` dispatch with a probe scope so Goal1
86	reports per-dispatch latency alongside message count.
87	
88	- `Cargo.toml`: version bump to 0.1.0-2; add `hdrhistogram = "7"` and
89	  `minstant = "0.1"` dependencies.
90	- `src/perf/mod.rs`: new module root; documents vendor source path
91	  (`../iiac-perf/src`) and divergences (import paths, `// OK: …`
92	  on `unwrap*`, one diagnostic string).
93	- `src/perf/tprobe2.rs`: vendored from `iiac-perf/src/tprobe2.rs`.
94	  Imports rewritten to `crate::perf::{band_table, ticks}`; two
95	  `unwrap` calls gained `// OK: …` justifications.
96	- `src/perf/band_table.rs`: vendored from `iiac-perf/src/band_table.rs`.
97	  Imports rewritten to `crate::perf::fmt` and `crate::perf::ticks`;
98	  `unwrap_or(0)` calls gained `// OK: …` justifications.
99	- `src/perf/ticks.rs`: vendored unchanged except for the
100	  `compile_error!` diagnostic now mentions `actor-x1 probes`.
101	- `src/perf/ticks/x86_64.rs`: vendored unchanged.
102	- `src/perf/fmt.rs`: extracted `fmt_commas` and `fmt_commas_f64`
103	  from `iiac-perf/src/harness.rs` (lines 160–188) — avoids vendoring
104	  the 393-line harness for two helpers. `unwrap_or(0)` gained a
105	  `// OK: …` justification.
106	- `src/lib.rs`: adds `pub mod perf;`.
107	- `src/runtime.rs`: `SingleThreadRuntime::new(probe_name)` now takes
108	  a name, embeds a `TProbe2`, and wraps each `handle_message` call
109	  with `probe.start(dst_id as u64)` / `probe.end(id)`. `Default` impl
110	  dropped (new takes an arg). `probe_mut()` exposes the probe for
111	  end-of-run reporting. Unit tests pass a probe name.
112	- `src/bin/goal1.rs`: new arg parser supports `-t` / `--ticks`
113	  (raw-ticks display); runtime constructed as `"goal1.dispatch"`;
114	  calls `rt.probe_mut().report(as_ticks)` after the dispatch loop.
115	  Usage errors now `exit(2)` with a usage line instead of panicking.
116	
117	### Smoke measurements (release build)
118	
119	- `goal1 0.5` — 10.56 M messages in 0.5s (~21.1 M msg/s).
120	  Per-dispatch: mean 9 ns, mean min-p99 9 ns, p99 max 67.5 µs
121	  (likely an OS interrupt in the tail).
122	- `goal1 0.5 -t` — 10.57 M messages in 0.5s (~21.1 M msg/s).
123	  Per-dispatch: mean 35 tk, mean min-p99 34 tk, p99 max 114,687 tk.
124	
125	Adding the probe cuts throughput roughly in half vs. 0.1.0-1
126	(~38.7 → ~21.1 M msg/s). The bot thinks the delta is dominated by
127	two `rdtsc` reads plus `Vec::push` per dispatch (~20 ns of the
128	~47 ns per-dispatch cost); the 9 ns measured by the probe is the
129	`handle_message` body alone.
130	
131	## Inner batching, warmup, clap (0.1.0-3)
132	
133	Adds CLI polish and measurement-quality improvements to Goal1.
134	Scope:
135	
136	- **Inner batching** — one probe scope now covers `--inner N`
137	  consecutive dispatches (default 1). At larger `N` the probe's
138	  two-`rdtsc`-plus-push apparatus cost is amortized, and stored
139	  tick values land in a range where the histogram's 0.1 %-relative
140	  buckets actually resolve jitter.
141	- **Warmup** — `--warmup SECS` (default **10.0**) runs the same
142	  dispatch loop with the probe active, then clears probe state;
143	  measurement phase inherits stable-state CPU freq / cache /
144	  branch-predictor conditions. Warmup message counts are never
145	  reported.
146	- **clap** — hand-rolled arg parsing replaced with a derive-based
147	  `Cli`. Validates non-negative finite `duration_s` and `warmup`;
148	  enforces `inner ≥ 1`. Auto-generated `--help` / `--version`.
149	- **TProbe2 additions** (vendored-code divergence from iiac-perf):
150	  - `Record` gains a `batch: u64` field.
151	  - `TProbe2::end_batch(id, n)` appends with `batch = n`; existing
152	    `end(id)` sets `batch = 1`.
153	  - `TProbe2::report` now divides stored deltas by `batch` at
154	    drain time so the histogram always holds per-event cost,
155	    regardless of scope granularity.
156	  - `TProbe2::clear()` resets records buffer and histogram;
157	    called between warmup and measurement.
158	
159	File-level edits:
160	
161	- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-3; add
162	  `clap = { version = "4", features = ["derive", "wrap_help"] }`.
163	- `src/perf/tprobe2.rs`: `Record.batch`, `end_batch`, `clear`,
164	  per-event division in `report`. Doc comment at file head lists
165	  divergences from upstream. Three new tests
166	  (`end_batch_stores_batch_size`, `report_divides_batched_delta`,
167	  `clear_empties_records_and_hist`).
168	- `src/runtime.rs`: `run_for(duration, inner)` wraps each batch
169	  of `inner` dispatches in a probe scope; site_id is the first
170	  `dst_id` in the batch. Partial trailing batches (queue empties
171	  mid-scope) are discarded — scope dropped, count not incremented
172	  — so throughput numbers reflect whole-batch work only. New test
173	  `partial_trailing_batch_is_dropped` covers the drop path.
174	- `src/bin/goal1.rs`: clap-derive `Cli` with `duration_s`,
175	  `--warmup`, `--inner`, `-t/--ticks`. Runs warmup, clears probe,
176	  runs measurement, prints count/throughput (now also reports
177	  `inner=N`), then calls `probe.report`.
178	
179	### Smoke measurements (release, `--warmup 0`)
180	
181	| Args | Throughput | Probe mean (min-p99) | Notes |
182	|---|---|---|---|
183	| `0.5` (inner=1) | 19.7 M msg/s | 9 ns | Apparatus overhead dominates, distribution stuck in two spike buckets |
184	| `0.5 --inner 1000` | 205 M msg/s | 5 ns | ~10× throughput; per-dispatch mean drops because apparatus is amortized |
185	| `0.5 --inner 1000 -t` | 204 M msg/s | 18 tk | Ticks view; tail p99-max 449 tk vs ~115 k tk at inner=1 (batch smooths OS interrupts) |
186	
187	Warmup wall-clock sanity: `goal1 0.3 --warmup 1 --inner 1000` ran
188	in 1.33s total. Argv error paths (`-1`, `--inner 0`, `--warmup nan`)
189	all exit 2 with clap-formatted messages.
190	
191	The bot thinks the inner=1 vs inner=1000 gap (9 ns vs 5 ns
192	per-event) is ~4 ns of apparatus overhead; 0.1.0-5's overhead
193	calibration will confirm that estimate directly.
194	
195	## Always render all 12 percentile bands (0.1.0-4)
196	
197	Previously the renderer skipped any band where no hdrhistogram
198	bucket was assigned by mid-rank (`if band_count[i] == 0 { continue;
199	}`), so tight distributions produced reports with only 3–5 visible
200	rows and it was hard to tell at a glance whether a band was
201	genuinely empty or just not rendered. This step always emits all
202	12 rows (`min-p1` through `p99-max`); empty bands render as zeros
203	in every numeric column.
204	
205	File-level edits:
206	
207	- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-4.
208	- `src/perf/band_table.rs`: drop the skip-on-empty guard; the band
209	  loop now computes `(first_v, last_v, range_v, mean_v)` from
210	  either the band aggregates (for non-empty bands) or `(0, 0, 0, 0)`
211	  for empty ones. `unwrap_or(0)` `// OK: …` justifications updated
212	  ("rows always contains n_bands=12 entries").
213	
214	### Display example (inner=10, 0.5 s)
215	
216	All 12 rows always visible — the zero-valued bands make the
217	distribution's shape explicit:
218	
219	```
220	    min-p1        4 ns        4 ns        0 ns        8,712     4 ns
221	    p1-p10        0 ns        0 ns        0 ns            0     0 ns
222	    ...
223	    p40-p50       5 ns        5 ns        0 ns    4,794,952     5 ns
224	    ...
225	    p99-max       7 ns    3,197 ns    3,190 ns       61,924    10 ns
226	```
227	
228	### Caveat — this only fixes visibility, not the bucket-into-band
229	mapping
230	
231	The renderer still walks hdrhistogram buckets and assigns each
232	bucket to exactly one band by cumulative mid-rank. In the inner=10
233	smoke above, 4,794,952 samples (~89 %) land in `p40-p50` while
234	`p1-p10` through `p30-p40` stay at zero — the single hot bucket at
235	"5 ns" got attributed to one band, not spread across the percentile
236	range its samples actually span. The bot thinks the principled
237	fix (switch to `hist.value_at_quantile(q)` per band boundary) is
238	worth considering if the zero-band display starts feeling
239	misleading; deferred for now.
240	
241	### Divergence from upstream
242	
243	Further modification of the vendored `src/perf/band_table.rs`.
244	Noted in the perf module's divergence list; already has other
245	actor-x1-specific edits (`// OK:` comments, import paths).
246	
247	## CLI polish: help width + version banner (0.1.0-5)
248	
249	Two small quality-of-life tweaks to the `goal1` CLI.
250	
251	### Help width cap at 80 cols
252	
253	clap's default help-width uses the terminal width, which on wide
254	terminals produces hard-to-scan long lines for the `--warmup` and
255	`--inner` descriptions. `max_term_width = 80` caps rendering at
256	80 columns while still using terminal width if smaller.
257	
258	### Version banner
259	
260	First line of `goal1` output — and of `goal1 -h` — is now
261	`{CARGO_PKG_NAME} {CARGO_PKG_VERSION}` (e.g. `actor-x1 0.1.0-5`),
262	matching what `--version` already prints. Helps identify which
263	build produced a given result sheet (or which help text a user is
264	reading) when lines are pasted out of context. The existing
265	`goal1: …` line below carries the binary identity, so the banner
266	doesn't repeat "goal1". Implemented in two places: a `println!`
267	at the top of `main` for normal runs, and clap's `before_help`
268	attribute for help output (`concat!(env!(CARGO_PKG_NAME), " ",
269	env!(CARGO_PKG_VERSION))` — compile-time concat of package env
270	vars).
271	
272	### File-level edits
273	
274	- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-5.
275	- `src/bin/goal1.rs`: extend the `Cli` struct's `#[command(...)]`
276	  attribute with `max_term_width = 80` and
277	  `before_help = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"))`;
278	  `println!` the same banner at the top of `main` for
279	  non-help runs.
280	
281	## Apparatus overhead calibration (0.1.0-6)
282	
283	Adds a calibration phase between warmup and measurement that fits
284	the probe's framing cost (the bias on every stored
285	`end_tsc − start_tsc` delta) and subtracts the appropriate per-
286	event correction at report time. `--raw` opts out.
287	
288	### Why two-point (not single-point)
289	
290	`rdtsc` isn't a serializing instruction, so two back-to-back reads
291	with nothing between can execute out-of-order and measure ~0 ticks
292	— below the true apparatus cost. `_mm_lfence` bracketing drains
293	the pipeline harder than real work does (measured 38 tk on this
294	box, 2× the real bias). The two-point fit gives the right answer
295	by varying `inner` on an empty bench and solving
296	`raw_delta = framing + inner · loop_per_iter` in `f64` (no
297	integer-division loss at small values).
298	
299	On this box the fit lands on **framing = 21 tk (~5.5 ns)**,
300	reproducible across runs. With the correction applied,
301	`inner=1` probe mean drops from 9 ns (raw) to 4 ns, which matches
302	the `inner=1000` raw mean of 5 ns (where the correction is
303	negligible) — both phases converge on the same actual
304	`handle_message` cost.
305	
306	### File-level edits
307	
308	- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-6.
309	- `src/perf/overhead.rs`: new module. Constants `CAL_WARMUP = 10_000`,
310	  `CAL_SAMPLES = 1_000`, `N_LOW = 100`, `N_HIGH = 10_000`. `Overhead`
311	  struct carries `framing_ticks`, `loop_per_iter_ticks`,
312	  `cal_raw_low_ticks`, `cal_raw_high_ticks`, `cal_duration`.
313	  `calibrate()` runs the fit; `Overhead::per_event_ticks(batch)`
314	  returns `framing_ticks / batch.max(1)` for subtraction. Two unit
315	  tests (amortization math + calibration smoke).
316	- `src/perf/mod.rs`: `pub mod overhead` + re-export
317	  `Overhead`/`calibrate`.
318	- `src/perf/tprobe2.rs`: `TProbe2::report` signature gains
319	  `overhead: Option<&Overhead>`; drain loop subtracts
320	  `ovh.per_event_ticks(batch)` from each `per_event_raw` before
321	  histogram insertion (via `saturating_sub`, clamp at 1). New
322	  test `report_subtracts_overhead`. Old tests updated to pass `None`.
323	- `src/bin/goal1.rs`: new `--raw` flag; calibrates on the warmed
324	  system between `probe.clear()` and the measurement `run_for`;
325	  prints an `apparatus: …` diagnostic line before the band-table
326	  report.
327	
328	### What's *not* subtracted
329	
330	Only `framing_ticks`. `loop_per_iter_ticks` (from the black_box
331	empty bench) is kept on `Overhead` for diagnostic visibility but
332	deliberately not subtracted — the real dispatch loop's per-iter
333	cost (queue pop, deadline check, SingleCtx construction, dynamic
334	dispatch) is part of what the user wants to measure, and
335	`black_box(1)`'s loop_per_iter doesn't model it anyway. User
336	already signed off on this scope.
337	
338	### Smoke
339	
340	- `goal1 0.5 --warmup 0 --inner 1` — 19.9 M msg/s, apparatus
341	  21 tk / 5.54 ns, probe mean min-p99 **4 ns** (from 9 ns raw).
342	- `goal1 0.5 --warmup 0 --inner 1000` — 204 M msg/s, apparatus
343	  correction 0 tk (rounds down), probe mean min-p99 5 ns
344	  (unchanged — raw already clean at this batch).
345	- `goal1 0.5 --warmup 0 --inner 1 --raw` — apparatus: "raw (no
346	  overhead subtraction)"; no calibration run.
347	- 14/14 tests pass. Calibration adds ~10 ms to default runs.
348	
349	## Goal2: per-actor threads + mpsc channels (0.1.0-7)
350	
351	Implements Goal2 of Stage 1: two actors running on their own
352	threads, ping-ponging an empty [`Message`] over `std::sync::mpsc`
353	channels, with the same warmup/measurement/calibration lifecycle
354	as goal1.
355	
356	### Design decisions
357	
358	- **Factory closures instead of pre-boxed actors.** `add_actor`
359	  takes `FnOnce() -> A` where `A: Actor + Send + 'static`; the
360	  factory runs inside the actor's own thread. Keeps the [`Actor`]
361	  trait free of a `Send` bound and matches how real actor systems
362	  typically spawn state.
363	- **In-band shutdown via internal `Signal` enum.** Channel payload
364	  is `Signal { User(Message), ClearProbe, Shutdown }`, not
365	  `Message` directly. Main thread injects `ClearProbe` at the
366	  warmup → measurement boundary and `Shutdown` after measurement;
367	  actors match on the variant in their `recv()` loop. Avoids an
368	  out-of-band atomic flag and the associated polling.
369	- **`inner` fixed at 1.** Ping-pong keeps at most one message in
370	  flight per channel, so batched probe scopes (`inner > 1`) could
371	  never fill — every scope would hit the partial-batch drop path.
372	  The `--inner` flag from goal1 is not exposed; the hardcoded
373	  `inner=1` is reflected in the throughput line.
374	- **Per-actor probes, merged reporting.** Each actor thread owns
375	  a [`TProbe2`] named `"goal2.dispatch.actor{id}"`. Main collects
376	  `(count, probe)` on join and prints a per-actor band table (not
377	  merged — two independent distributions are more informative
378	  than one blended histogram in a ping-pong workload where the
379	  actors should be near-identical).
380	- **Calibration pre-spawn on main thread.** Matches goal1's
381	  calibration code path. Same CPU → same `Overhead` applies to
382	  all actor threads. Could be refined later to calibrate
383	  per-thread, but unnecessary for Stage 1.
384	
385	### File-level edits
386	
387	- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-7.
388	- `src/runtime.rs`: add `MultiThreadRuntime`, `Signal` enum,
389	  `ActorFactory` alias, `MultiCtx`, and `actor_loop` helper below
390	  the existing single-thread code. New `#[cfg(test)] mod mt_tests`
391	  with a ping-pong smoke test (50 ms warmup + 50 ms measurement).
392	- `src/bin/goal2.rs`: new binary mirroring goal1's CLI shape
393	  (version banner, help width cap, --warmup, -t/--ticks, --raw),
394	  no --inner. Constructs `MultiThreadRuntime`, adds two
395	  `PingPongActor` factories, seeds one message, calls
396	  `rt.run(warmup, measurement)`, prints summary + apparatus +
397	  per-actor reports.
398	
399	### Smoke (release, `--warmup 0`)
400	
401	- `goal2 0.5` — 116,495 messages in 0.5 s (~0.23 M msg/s).
402	  Mean per-dispatch ~1.17 µs; median ~1.34 µs; p99 tail to
403	  ~22 µs (mostly scheduling jitter). Actors near-symmetric
404	  (58,247 vs 58,248 messages).
405	- `goal2 -h` — wraps at 80 cols, shows the `actor-x1 0.1.0-7`
406	  banner, no `--inner` flag.
407	- `goal2 0.2 --raw` — apparatus: `raw (no overhead subtraction)`;
408	  calibration skipped.
409	- goal1 unchanged: still ~200 M msg/s at `--inner 1000`.
410	- Tests: 15/15 pass (adds `multi_thread_runs_measures_and_shuts_down_cleanly`).
411	
412	### The ~250× single-thread gap
413	
414	Goal1 at `inner=1000` hits ~205 M msg/s (~5 ns/dispatch);
415	goal2 at `inner=1` hits ~0.23 M msg/s (~4 µs/round-trip, ~1.2 µs
416	per-event inside the probe scope). The bot thinks the gap is
417	dominated by: (1) mpsc's `Mutex` + condvar wake for every
418	message, (2) two cross-thread context switches per ping-pong
419	round, (3) cache-line bouncing between the two actors' threads.
420	An unbounded channel or a lock-free mpsc implementation would
421	close some of this but the context-switch floor is fundamental.
422	
423	## `--pin` for thread CPU affinity (0.1.0-8)
424	
425	Adds `--pin <CORES>` to both binaries. Tightens the stdev of
426	probe reports by eliminating OS thread-migration noise, at the
427	cost of exposing placement sensitivity in goal2 (pinning to
428	cross-CCX cores raises the mean because every message forces a
429	cross-complex cache coherence round-trip).
430	
431	Adapted from `../iiac-perf/src/pin.rs` with only the two
432	primitives we need (`parse_cores`, `pin_current`); the affinity
433	save/restore, `--no-pin-cal`, and mask-summary helpers are
434	elided — Stage 1 doesn't need them.
435	
436	### File-level edits
437	
438	- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-8; add `core_affinity = "0.8"`.
439	- `src/perf/pin.rs`: new module. `parse_cores(&str) -> Result<Vec<usize>, String>`
440	  (comma-separated / range list, duplicates preserved for
441	  oversubscription) and `pin_current(Option<usize>)` (pin the
442	  calling thread; no-op on `None`). 7 unit tests for the parser
443	  (plain list, range, mixed, duplicates, empty string, reverse
444	  range errors, garbage errors).
445	- `src/perf/mod.rs`: `pub mod pin`.
446	- `src/runtime.rs`: `MultiThreadRuntime::run` gains a
447	  `pin_cores: &[usize]` parameter; actor `i` pins to
448	  `pin_cores[i % pin_cores.len()]` inside its spawned thread
449	  (before the actor factory runs or the probe is created). Empty
450	  slice = unpinned (existing behavior). The lone mt_test updated
451	  to pass `&[]`.
452	- `src/bin/goal1.rs`: `--pin <CORES>` flag (`Option<String>`;
453	  parsed via `pin::parse_cores` in `main`). Uses the first core
454	  of the list (goal1 is single-threaded); extra cores silently
455	  ignored. Pins the main thread before calibration and dispatch.
456	  New `pinning: …` line in the output.
457	- `src/bin/goal2.rs`: `--pin <CORES>` flag, same parsing. Parsed
458	  list passed through to `rt.run(warmup, measurement, &pins)`.
459	  `pinning: actor0→coreN, actor1→coreM, …` line in the output.
460	
461	### A/B on this box (3900X, 12-core / 24-thread)
462	
463	`goal2 0.5 --warmup 0.5` — same workload, three placements:
464	
465	| Pinning     | Throughput | Mean / event | Mean min-p99 | Stdev min-p99 |
466	|---          |---         |---           |---           |---            |
467	| unpinned    | 0.374 M/s  | 632 ns       | 622 ns       | **246 ns**    |
468	| `--pin 0,1` | 0.308 M/s  | 667 ns       | 654 ns       | 171 ns        |
469	| `--pin 0,4` | 0.212 M/s  | 1,496 ns     | 1,487 ns     | **80 ns**     |
470	
471	**Stdev tightens dramatically** with pinning: min-p99 stdev
472	drops 246 → 80 ns (3×) at `--pin 0,4`. Migration noise was a
473	real component of the unpinned variance.
474	
475	**p10–p40 rises** (user's prediction): `--pin 0,4` puts the two
476	actors on different CCX complexes (each CCX = 6 cores on Zen 2),
477	so every ping-pong round-trip incurs a cross-CCX cache coherence
478	transfer. Mean climbs from 632 ns → 1,496 ns — the placement cost.
479	`--pin 0,1` (same CCX) stays close to unpinned throughput
480	(~0.31 M/s vs 0.37) but still narrows stdev somewhat.
481	
482	The bot thinks these numbers are the intended story: pinning
483	trades a potentially higher mean for much more reproducible
484	measurements, which is the right tradeoff for code-change A/B
485	work. The placement itself then becomes a tunable — users pick
486	cores that match what they want to measure (best-case
487	same-complex, worst-case cross-socket, etc.).
488	
489	### `goal1 --pin`
490	
491	Single-thread goal1 sees little throughput change with
492	`--pin 5` (~206 M msg/s, identical to unpinned) — all the work
493	is on one thread anyway, and migration cost is already amortized
494	over the 200 M msg/s inner loop. Value of pinning is purely
495	reproducibility.
496

> DEVELOPER

I want to extract "perf" into tprobe a tprobe module, thoughts

> AGENT

Three flavors to pick between — main tradeoff is how much boundary you want:

1. **In-tree rename** (`src/perf/` → `src/tprobe/`): trivial, one commit, pure namespace change. Almost no benefit over status quo.
2. **Workspace crate** inside this repo (`tprobe/` as a workspace member, `actor-x1` depends on it by path): forces a clean `pub` surface, reusable across future bins/tests, still one repo so refactors are atomic. The bot thinks this is the right next step — Stage 2 is the trigger the 0.1.0-2 chores note called out, and all the divergence from `iiac-perf` has settled.
3. **External sibling crate** (`../tprobe/`): premature — API isn't stable (Stage 2 may rework `Overhead` or add per-thread calibration), and cross-repo coupling is exactly what we avoided by vendoring in the first place.

Recommendation: (2), workspace split, as a multi-step plan (`0.2.0-0` marker → move files → rewire `actor-x1` to depend on path → final `0.2.0`). Want me to draft that plan, or did you mean one of the other two?

> DEVELOPER

2

> AGENT

Before I draft the ladder, two decisions to lock in:

**Q1 — Layout.** Two options:
- **(a) Mixed root**: keep `Cargo.toml` as `[package]` for `actor-x1` AND add `[workspace]`. `tprobe/` is a subdir member. `actor-x1`'s source stays in `src/`. Minimal churn.
- **(b) Virtual root**: convert root to `[workspace]`-only with `crates/actor-x1/` and `crates/tprobe/`. Cleaner symmetric layout, but moves every `actor-x1` file.

Recommendation: **(a)**. The extraction is about splitting `perf/` out cleanly; moving all of `actor-x1`'s files at the same time is unrelated churn and bigger blast radius.

**Q2 — Naming.** The crate is `tprobe`, but the star type is `TProbe2` and the file is `tprobe2.rs`. Keep the "2" (historical, matches upstream `iiac-perf`), or drop it inside the new crate (so the crate root re-exports `TProbe`)?

Recommendation: **drop the "2"**. Inside the new crate, `TProbe2` → `TProbe`, `tprobe2.rs` → `tprobe.rs`. The "2" only meant "second try" inside `iiac-perf`; in its own crate it's just noise. Trivially re-added if you disagree. (Divergence from upstream is already substantial — one more isn't costly.)

**Draft ladder** (assuming (a) + drop "2", multi-step):

| Version | Landmark |
|---|---|
| `0.2.0-0` | Plan marker. Add `[workspace]` to root `Cargo.toml`; create empty `tprobe/` skeleton (`Cargo.toml` + `src/lib.rs` stub). No file moves. |
| `0.2.0-1` | Move `src/perf/*` → `tprobe/src/*`. Rewrite internal imports (`crate::perf::x` → `crate::x`). Move `hdrhistogram`/`minstant`/`core_affinity` deps from root to `tprobe/Cargo.toml`. Add `tprobe = { path = "tprobe" }` to `actor-x1` deps. Rewire call sites in `runtime.rs`, `goal1.rs`, `goal2.rs` (`crate::perf::x` → `tprobe::x`). Drop `pub mod perf;` from `src/lib.rs`. Tests + binaries unchanged in behavior. |

```
200	## Description (X.Y.Z)
201	```
202	
203	Example: `` ## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0) ``
204	
205	### Pre-commit checklist
206	
207	Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
208	
209	1. `cargo fmt`
210	2. `cargo clippy`
211	3. `cargo test`
212	4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
213	5. Retest after install
214	6. Update `notes/todo.md` — add to `## Done` if completing a task
215	7. Update `notes/chores-*.md` — add a subsection describing the change
216	8. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new flags,
217	   new subcommands, changed behavior)
218	
219	## Code Conventions
220	
221	### Doc comments on every file, function, and method
222	
223	Every `.rs` file must begin with a `//!` module docstring. Every
224	function and method must have a `///` doc comment. Keep them brief —
225	one sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the
226	comment exists, not that it be long.
227	
228	This is a deliberate override of the generic "write no comments"
229	default that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the
230	module / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments
231	inside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a
232	non-obvious WHY.
233	
234	### `// OK: …` comments on `unwrap*` calls (Rust)
235	
236	Non-test code that calls `.unwrap()`, `.unwrap_or(…)`,
237	`.unwrap_or_default()`, or `.unwrap_or_else(…)` must have a trailing
238	`// OK: …` comment that justifies why the call is acceptable.
239	
240	- `// OK: <specific reason>` — document the real precondition,
241	  invariant, or domain reason. Preferred whenever the reason isn't
242	  self-evident.
243	- `// OK: obvious` — the default is self-evident from context (e.g.
244	  `desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("")` — empty desc → empty title).
245	
246	Bare `// OK` is not used (reads like a truncated comment).
247	Abbreviations (e.g. `SE`) are not used because they require a decoder
248	ring for readers seeing the code out of context.
249	
250	For provably-unreachable `.unwrap()` calls, also prefix with
251	`#[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]` so the site stays silent if we enable
252	the project-wide `clippy::unwrap_used` lint later.
253	
254	```rust
255	let max = stderr_level.unwrap_or(LevelFilter::Info); // OK: default verbosity when -v/-vv absent
256	let first_line = desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("");  // OK: obvious
257	
258	match matches.len() {
259	    1 => {
260	        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
261	        // OK: `1 =>` arm guarantees matches.len() == 1
262	        Ok(TitleMatch::One(matches.into_iter().next().unwrap()))
263	    }
264	    // ...
265	}
266	```
267	
268	Tests (`#[cfg(test)]`) are exempt — panicking on setup failure is the
269	correct test behavior.
270	
271	### Ask for clarification on ambiguous input
272	
273	When user input is ambiguous or missing necessary detail, stop and
274	ask a specific question. Do not proceed on a guess and hope the
275	result lands right — a clarifying question costs a few seconds;
276	redoing misaligned work costs much more.
277	
278	### Recognize when stuck
279	
280	If a simple task has eaten 5+ minutes of thinking or back-and-forth
281	without progress, stop. Summarize what's blocking — unclear
282	requirements, unfamiliar API surface, conflicting signals — and ask.
283	Continued flailing produces worse outcomes than a direct "I'm stuck
284	on X."
285	
286	## ochid Trailers
287	
288	Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
289	counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
290	path followed by the changeID:
291	
292	- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
293	- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`
294	
295	Use `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` to get both changeIDs (first line
296	is app repo, second is `.claude`).
297	
298	## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow
299	
300	**Use `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` — it wraps the full flow in one
301	command with two interactive approval gates.** Push runs
302	preflight (sync + fmt + clippy + test), prompts for review,
303	composes the commit message (via `--title`/`--body` or `$EDITOR`),
304	prompts for message approval, then commits both repos → advances
305	bookmarks → pushes app → finalizes `.claude`. Failures inside the
306	local mutation window roll both repos back via `jj op restore`;
307	after `push-app` succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
308	recovery is forward-only.
309	
310	**Run this flow after every step** — not only at session end.
311	Single-step and multi-step changes are of equal importance: a
312	single-step change is one `push` invocation; a multi-step change
313	is one `push` per `X.Y.Z-N` commit plus one for the final release
314	commit. Each step gets its own commit, its own push, and its own
315	finalize — so dev markers land on the remote and in `.claude` as
316	they happen rather than being batched until the end.
317	
318	### Run `vc-x1 push`
319	
320	```
321	vc-x1 push main                                      # interactive (review + $EDITOR)
322	vc-x1 push main --title "..." --body "..."           # flags skip $EDITOR
323	vc-x1 push main --yes --title "..." --body "..."     # full non-interactive
324	vc-x1 push main --dry-run                            # preview (no side effects)
325	vc-x1 push main --from commit-app                    # resume from specific stage
326	vc-x1 push --status                                  # show saved state
327	vc-x1 push main --restart                            # clear saved state; start fresh
328	```
329	
330	The two approval gates are surfaced by push itself:
331	
332	1. **Review** — push prints `jj diff --stat` for both repos and
333	   prompts `[y/N]`. Approve = "the work is done right".
334	2. **Message** — push either uses `--title`/`--body` (non-editor
335	   path) or opens `$EDITOR` on a template. Approve = "the message
336	   reads right".
337	
338	Both titles and bodies are the **same** across the two commits;
339	only the `ochid:` trailer differs per repo. Push collects the
340	pre-commit chids internally so you don't hand-manage them.
341	
342	For the full flag list and stage machine, see `vc-x1 push --help`
343	and `notes/chores-05.md > Add push subcommand (0.37.0)`.
344	
345	### Bot communication during the flow
346	
347	When applying the flow on the user's behalf, use plain prose at
348	each gate — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal", "Checkpoint N",
349	etc.):
350	
351	1. **After completing the work** — summarize what changed
352	   (file-by-file or feature-by-feature, terse) and end with
353	   something like:
354	
355	   > Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the
356	   > commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.
357	
358	2. **After review approval** — present the proposed `$TITLE` and
359	   `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to run `vc-x1 push`.
360	   Do **not** spell out the full
361	   `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title "$TITLE" --body "$BODY"`
362	   invocation by default — it's mechanical and obvious from the
363	   title/body. Show it only on explicit request (verbose mode /
364	   debugging).
365	
366	3. **After execution approval** — run the push command. `push`
367	   handles commit + bookmark + push + finalize internally;
368	   nothing should be output after the push command (finalize is
369	   detached and absolute-last; see "After finalize: stop and
370	   wait" below).
371	
372	### Pre-step: `vc-x1 sync` (still useful)
373	
374	Push's preflight runs `vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run` as its first step
375	so divergence is resolved before the build. Running sync manually
376	before you *start* editing is still cheap (one line when clean)
377	and surfaces remote changes earlier:
378	
379	```
380	vc-x1 sync                            # dual-repo workspace
381	vc-x1 sync -R .                       # single-repo project
382	vc-x1 sync --quiet                    # silent; exit code signals result
383	```
384	
385	Output shape:
386	
387	- **Clean**: one line — `sync: N repos, all up-to-date`. Proceed.
388	- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`): per-repo fetch +
389	  state lines, then `dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply`.
390	  Inspect, then re-run with `--no-dry-run` (or resolve conflicts
391	  if rebase fails).
392	- **`--quiet`**: no output; exit code is the only signal.
393	
394	### After finalize: stop and wait
395	
396	After `vc-x1 finalize` is launched — **whether mid-session per-step
397	or at session end** — you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit
398	files, run tools, or emit any text (prose, recaps, acknowledgements),
399	until the user explicitly directs you to continue. Treat finalize as
400	a hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words (e.g. "next is ...")
401	must be said in the approval prompt *before* executing finalize; the
402	finalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the turn and nothing
403	follows it.
404	
405	This holds even when the next step seems obvious (e.g. "next is
406	N+1" or "now I should bump the version and commit the release").
407	Wait. The user controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint
408	they may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a break at.
409	Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint and produces unwanted writes
410	between finalize and the next explicit instruction.
411	
412	Exceptions to this rule may emerge later but are not authorized at
413	this stage. Until told otherwise, treat as absolute.
414	
415	### Late changes after push
416	
417	If the app repo needs a tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.
418	updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is immutable. Use
419	`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:
420	
421	```
422	jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
423	jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
424	jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
425	```
426	
427	(`.claude` is also mutable via this pattern when needed, though
428	push's `finalize-claude` stage normally handles trailing session
429	writes so you rarely hit this case there.)
430	
431	### Manual finalize fallback
432	
433	If push exited before `finalize-claude` (e.g. `--no-finalize`
434	was set, or a failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`),
435	run finalize by hand:
436	
437	```
438	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
439	```
440	
441	**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no
442	tool calls, no additional output. If any work is done after
443	finalize, run finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
444	Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
445	already displays it.
446	
```

784	fn stage_preflight(root: &Path, args: &PushArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
785	    if args.dry_run {
786	        info!(

788	        );
789	        return Ok(());

793	    info!("push:preflight: cargo fmt");

806	/// Review: first approval gate — "is the work done right?".
807	///
808	/// Shows a `jj diff --stat` of the pending changes in both repos
809	/// and prompts the user to continue. `--yes` short-circuits the
810	/// prompt (required for scripted / non-tty use). In `--dry-run`
811	/// the diff is still shown (that's the point of dry-run — see
812	/// what *would* be reviewed) but approval is auto-granted.
813	fn stage_review(root: &Path, args: &PushArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
814	    let claude = claude_path(root);
815	    let app_arg = root.to_string_lossy();
816	    let claude_arg = claude.to_string_lossy();
817	    info!("push:review: pending changes:");
818	    info!("  app ({app_arg}):");
819	    let app_stat = run("jj", &["diff", "--stat", "-R", &app_arg], root)?;
820	    for line in app_stat.lines() {
821	        info!("    {line}");
822	    }
823	    info!("  .claude ({claude_arg}):");
824	    let claude_stat = run("jj", &["diff", "--stat", "-R", &claude_arg], root)?;
825	    for line in claude_stat.lines() {
826	        info!("    {line}");
827	    }
828	    if args.yes {
829	        info!("push:review: auto-approved (--yes)");
830	        return Ok(());
831	    }
832	    if args.dry_run {
833	        info!("push:review: [dry-run] auto-approved");
834	        return Ok(());
835	    }
836	    if !is_stdin_tty() {
837	        return Err("push:review: stdin is not a tty; \
838	                    pass --yes to auto-approve in non-interactive contexts"
839	            .into());

> AGENT

Source-code findings (all three answers grounded in `vc-x1/src/push.rs`):

- **Preflight (push.rs:784–803)**: runs `vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run`, `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test`. Nothing else.
- **No `cargo install` / retest in preflight** (push.rs:780–781, explicit comment: *"pre-commit checklist (minus `cargo install` / retest, which are project-specific)"*). So step 4 stays on the bot.
- **Ochid trailer auto-appended** at the commit stages: `commit-app` appends `\n\nochid: /.claude/<claude_chid>` (push.rs:997); `commit-claude` appends `\n\nochid: /<app_chid>` (push.rs:1060). Template comment at push.rs:940: *"Body goes after a blank line. ochid trailers are appended"*. So the body passed via `--body` must **not** include an ochid trailer.
- **`--yes`** auto-approves review (push.rs:828–830) and message gates.

Both `actor-x1/CLAUDE.md` and `vc-template-x1/CLAUDE.md` are byte-identical — one diff applies to both.

**Proposed CLAUDE.md edits** (three, for agreement before applying):

**Edit A — `## Commit Message Style`** (around lines 112–125). Currently two bullets (`App-repo body` / `Session-repo body`) describe different bodies. Replace with three bullets describing the unified body + optional session-notes tail + ochid rule:

- **Body** (same across both repos): short intro paragraph (1–3 sentences), then a terse bullet list. Each bullet = one file, one-line gist, mirroring `notes/chores-*.md`. Chores is the source of truth; the body is a scan-able index.
- **Session notes** (optional tail appended to the body): if the session produced items worth preserving that don't fit the code-change index — in-session tooling work, dead ends, rationale — append a trailing subsection titled `## .claude session notes:` with those items as bullets. Same body (main content + any session-notes) lands in both commits.
- **Ochid trailer**: do *not* include `ochid:` in the body you pass via `--body`. Push appends the correct per-repo trailer at commit-app (`ochid: /.claude/<chid>`) and commit-claude (`ochid: /<chid>`).

**Edit B — `### Pre-commit checklist`** (around lines 205–217). Keep the 8-step list as-is, but append a short clarifying paragraph:

> Push's preflight re-runs `vc-x1 sync` + `cargo fmt` + `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` + `cargo test` as a safety net. Run the checklist above locally anyway — fmt/clippy/test iteration is cheaper in-conversation than bouncing off preflight after review approval. Steps 4–5 (`cargo install --path .` and retest) are **not** in push's preflight (project-specific); the bot runs them.

**Edit C — `### Bot communication during the flow`** (around lines 338–340). Currently says:

> Both titles and bodies are the **same** across the two commits; only the `ochid:` trailer differs per repo. Push collects the pre-commit chids internally so you don't hand-manage them.

Minor trim to cross-reference Commit Message Style rather than duplicate the body rules:

> Both titles and bodies are the **same** across the two commits; per-repo `ochid:` trailers are appended by push itself. See `## Commit Message Style` for body structure.

Agree on A / B / C? Any wording you'd tighten before I apply to both files?

100	## Commit Message Style
101	
102	Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
103	a version suffix:
104	
105	```
106	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
107	```
108	
109	- **Title**: target ~50 chars, short summary of *what* changed.
110	  Include the version. Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`,
111	  `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
112	- **App-repo body**: short intro paragraph (1–3 sentences), then a
113	  terse bullet list. Each bullet corresponds one-to-one with the
114	  edits structure already documented in `notes/chores-*.md` for
115	  this step — just the file and a one-line gist (e.g.
116	  `README.md: new Overview intro`). Do *not* restate the detail
117	  that lives in chores; the commit body is a scan-able index, not
118	  a duplicate. The chores section is the source of truth.
119	- **Session-repo body**: terse intro + a few session-activity
120	  bullets. Doesn't need to mirror chores since it describes
121	  in-session work, not code changes.
122	- Examples:
123	  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`
124	  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`
125	  - `refactor: deduplicate common CLI flags (0.21.1)`
126	
127	## Pre-commit Requirements
128	
129	### User approval
130	
131	Never execute commit, squash, push, or finalize commands without the
132	user's explicit approval. Present changes for review first; only run
133	them after the user confirms. This applies to late changes too —
134	pause for review before squashing into an existing commit.
135	
136	### Review before proposing the commit block
137	
138	After finishing a unit of work, **summarize what changed and stop
139	there**. Do not pre-emptively lay out the Checkpoint-1 commit
140	commands. Wait for the user to signal review is complete before
141	proposing the commit block. Changes during review are the norm,
142	not the exception; proposing commit text too early creates noise
143	and signals that I consider the work done when it usually isn't.
144	
145	This applies per-step in a multi-step flow too — each step gets a
146	review pause before its commit block appears.
147	
148	Signals that review is complete include explicit approval ("let's
149	commit", "looks good, commit it") **and any directive to start the
150	next step** ("do step 4", "next", "go N+1"). In that case the
151	previous step must be committed first — always commit the current
152	step before starting the next; don't ask.
153	
154	### Notes references
155	
156	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
157	See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
158	
159	### Versioning
160	
161	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based
162	on scope:
163	
164	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump
165	  directly to `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
166	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-0`,
167	  implement across multiple commits incrementing the numeric
168	  suffix. The final commit drops the suffix.
169	
170	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before
171	starting.
172	
173	For multi-step:
174	
175	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-0` with the plan and commit as a chore
176	   marker.
177	2. Implement in one or more `X.Y.Z-N` commits (increment N as
178	   needed).
179	3. Final commit bumps to `X.Y.Z` (no suffix), updates
180	   `notes/todo.md` and `notes/chores-*.md` — this is the "done"
181	   marker.
182	
183	**Why numeric suffixes (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:**
184	semver pre-release identifiers may consist of a single numeric
185	component, and they compare numerically per spec. So
186	`X.Y.Z-1 < X.Y.Z-2 < … < X.Y.Z` correctly orders the dev ladder
187	below the done marker. Cargo accepts this form. The `-dev` prefix
188	adds no information the git log doesn't already convey and
189	doubles typing per commit.
190	
191	The final release commit (no suffix) signals completion rather than
192	amending prior commits. This keeps history readable and makes it easy
193	to see which commits were exploratory vs final.
194	
195	### Chores section headers
196	
197	Chores section headers use trailing version format:
198	
199	```
200	## Description (X.Y.Z)
201	```
202	
203	Example: `` ## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0) ``
204	
205	### Pre-commit checklist
206	
207	Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
208	
209	1. `cargo fmt`
210	2. `cargo clippy`
211	3. `cargo test`
212	4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
213	5. Retest after install
214	6. Update `notes/todo.md` — add to `## Done` if completing a task
215	7. Update `notes/chores-*.md` — add a subsection describing the change
216	8. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new flags,
217	   new subcommands, changed behavior)
218	
219	## Code Conventions
220	
221	### Doc comments on every file, function, and method
222	
223	Every `.rs` file must begin with a `//!` module docstring. Every
224	function and method must have a `///` doc comment. Keep them brief —
225	one sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the
226	comment exists, not that it be long.
227	
228	This is a deliberate override of the generic "write no comments"
229	default that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the
```

Both CLAUDE.md files updated identically (diff empty).

**Summary of the three edits** (applied to `actor-x1/CLAUDE.md` and `vc-template-x1/CLAUDE.md`):

- **`## Commit Message Style`** — replaced the two divergent body bullets (App-repo / Session-repo) with three unified bullets: single shared **Body** (1–3 sentence intro + one-line-per-file bullet index mirroring chores), optional **Session notes** tail (`## .claude session notes:` subsection for items that don't fit the code-change index), explicit **Ochid trailer** rule (never include in `--body`; push appends per-repo).
- **`### Pre-commit checklist`** — kept the 8-step list; appended a clarifying paragraph: push's preflight re-runs sync/fmt/clippy/test as a safety net; run locally anyway; steps 4–5 (`cargo install` + retest) are project-specific, not in preflight, bot runs them.
- **`### Bot communication during the flow`** — trimmed the short "both bodies same" paragraph so body structure has one source of truth (Commit Message Style); it now cross-references instead of duplicating.

Note: these are late changes to `actor-x1/CLAUDE.md` *during* `0.2.0-0` — so they fold into the same working copy and ride along with the plan-marker commit (no separate commit needed). The `vc-template-x1` edit is independent and will need its own push in that repo, whenever you get to it.

> AGENT

Finding from the source: `push.rs:595-596` has `fn claude_path(root) -> root.join(".claude")` — **push hardcodes `.claude`**, while `fix_desc.rs`, `init.rs`, and friends actually read `[workspace].other-repo` from `.vc-config.toml` (and the template even emits `other-repo = ".."` in some modes, so the config is meant to vary). So CLAUDE.md is describing current reality for push, but the abstraction *is* real for the rest of the tool.

Three ways to clarify, ranked:

1. **Minimal (recommended)** — add a short "Terminology" block near the top defining `other-repo` as `[workspace].other-repo` from `.vc-config.toml` (default `.claude`, used as the concrete example throughout). Leave most existing `.claude` references alone, since they match what push actually does; only reword the few places where the label is conceptual (e.g. the session-notes subsection title could become `## <other-repo> session notes:` in description text, rendered literally as `## .claude session notes:` when composing for a `.claude`-default project).

2. **Full abstraction** — sweep `.claude` → `<other-repo>` throughout, add one "default is `.claude`" note. More correct-looking but misleading given push's hardcoding — readers may assume changing the config will Just Work.

3. **Fix push first, then (2)** — file a vc-x1 issue to teach push to read `other-repo` from config, then document the abstraction honestly. Bigger scope, probably right long-term.

The bot thinks (1) is the right call now: the abstraction exists for most of vc-x1 and is worth surfacing, but push's hardcoding means full abstraction in docs would overstate current behavior. Which flavor do you want — (1), (2), or (3)?

> AGENT

Push halted at `push-app`. Current state:

**Done locally:**
- App commit created: `oxtpuuon 33d15293 chore: plan workspace for this repo (0.2.0-0)`
- `.claude` commit created: `xvwtkuyo c1639253` (same title)
- Both `main` bookmarks advanced to the new commits

**Blocked at `push-app`:**
```
error: Error: Non-tracking remote bookmark main@origin exists
Hint: Run `jj bookmark track main --remote=origin` to import the remote bookmark.
```

**Two issues to resolve, want your call on both:**

1. **Remote tracking.** The hint suggests `jj bookmark track main@origin -R .`. Safe and reversible — just teaches jj to track the existing remote bookmark. After that, `vc-x1 push main --yes --from push-app` resumes the flow.

2. **`.vc-x1/push-state.toml` leaked into the app commit.** Push's own warning at the top said so: *"state dir '.vc-x1' is not in ... `.gitignore` — add a '/.vc-x1' line so in-progress push-state files don't get committed."* The review diff confirms: `.vc-x1/push-state.toml | 6 +` is part of commit `33d15293`. Options:

   - **(a) Fix forward**: add `/.vc-x1` to `.gitignore` as its own subsequent commit, push `33d15293` as-is. Simplest. Cost: one stale push-state file in history.
   - **(b) Rewrite the commit**: while it's still unpushed, `jj edit @-`, remove the file from the commit (via `jj file untrack` + delete from working copy), add `/.vc-x1` to `.gitignore` in the same commit, pop back with `jj new`. Clean history. `.claude` commit is untouched. More moving parts but nothing destructive if I'm careful.
   - **(c) Abandon and redo**: abandon both commits, fix `.gitignore` first, start push over. Cleanest reset, but we lose the `.claude` commit which was fine.

The bot thinks **(b)** is right — the commit isn't pushed yet so we can still rewrite it safely, and (a) would permanently bake a spurious state file into history. But (b) needs your approval before I touch the mutable commit, and if (b) goes wrong we fall back to (a).

Your call: (a) / (b) / (c), and go-ahead on the `jj bookmark track` step?

> DEVELOPER

Assuming you agree do both and squash them into the change and let's see how resuming works, push-state.toml has the current state so you should just be able to do `vc-x1 push` and it should "just-work". (Also, don't change vc-template-x1, that'll be on me). Your thoughts, don't do anything until we both agree.

> AGENT

Agreed on the approach. Analysis:

**Key insight:** the user's plan doesn't require removing `.vc-x1/push-state.toml` from the existing commit — it only requires `.gitignore` to exclude future changes. The file stays tracked in commit `33d15293` as a one-time scar, but once gitignored, subsequent modifications to it (as push advances through stages) won't leak into future commits. That keeps `push-state.toml` alive on disk for the resume, and keeps the amendment minimal.

**Proposed actions (in order):**

1. **Add `/.vc-x1` to `.gitignore`** (actor-x1 only, not vc-template-x1).

2. **Add a gitignore note to CLAUDE.md.** Best spot: a short "Prerequisites" paragraph near the top of `## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow`, before `### Run vc-x1 push`:

   > **Prerequisite.** `.gitignore` must include `/.vc-x1` so push's
   > in-progress state files (`.vc-x1/push-state.toml`) don't get
   > committed. Push warns at preflight if the entry is missing.
   > This only needs to be set up once per repo.

3. **Squash both changes into `@-`** (commit `33d15293`) via `jj squash -R .`. Since `/.vc-x1` is in `.gitignore` at snapshot time, the `push-state.toml` modifications push made during the failed run won't appear as tracked changes in `@`, so the squash carries only the two intended edits (`.gitignore` + `CLAUDE.md`). The already-tracked `push-state.toml` inside `33d15293` remains — scar accepted.

4. **Track the remote bookmark**: `jj bookmark track main@origin -R .`. Reversible, just teaches jj to track `main@origin`.

5. **Re-run push**: `vc-x1 push main --yes --title "..." --body "..."`. Push reads `.vc-x1/push-state.toml` (stage = `push-app`), resumes there, runs `push-app` → `finalize-claude`. Commits + bookmarks from the failed run stand.

**Risks I want to flag:**

- If `jj squash` somehow picks up push-state.toml changes in `@` (e.g. if push wrote to it after `.gitignore` was updated), those would end up in the squashed commit. The bot thinks this won't happen because push hasn't run since the original failure — but let's verify by inspecting `jj st` after the `.gitignore` + `CLAUDE.md` edits, before the squash.

- If the resumed `push-app` still fails (e.g. the `jj bookmark track` didn't fully resolve the issue), we're in the same spot as now — recoverable, just iterate.

- Push's preflight runs again on resume. If the squash produced any state that makes `cargo fmt`/`clippy`/`test` unhappy, it'll fail at preflight. These are doc-only edits though, so expected clean.

OK to proceed?

> AGENT

Two findings from the source:

**Finding 1 — the check exists, just in the wrong place.** `finalize.rs:158–229` has `find_non_tracking_remote` with the exact error message format we just hit (`"bookmark '<b>' has non-tracking remote '<b>@<remote>' — run jj bookmark track ..."`). So finalize pre-flights this. **Push's preflight does not** — `push.rs:784–803` only runs `sync` + `fmt` + `clippy` + `test`. That's inconsistent: push fails with a raw jj error at stage `push-app`, while finalize would refuse earlier with a clean message. The bot thinks moving (or duplicating) the check into push's preflight is a legit vc-x1 improvement worth filing.

**Finding 2 — no code path in sync or push untracks a bookmark.** I scanned both: no `jj bookmark forget`, no `jj bookmark untrack`, no `--no-track` anywhere. `sync` does `jj git fetch` + `jj bookmark set <b> -r <b>@<remote>` (fast-forward) / `jj rebase` — never `untrack` or `forget`. Push calls `jj bookmark set` and `jj git push --bookmark` — also never untracks.

**The bot thinks** this is the common jj behavior, not tool-caused: `jj git fetch` creates remote bookmarks as **non-tracking by default**. They only become tracked once someone runs `jj bookmark track <b>@<remote>` explicitly, and that state is then persisted. If this repo was initialized/cloned in a way that never called `track`, then all fetches since produce a non-tracking `main@origin` that any push will trip on. Sync doesn't untrack — it just never tracks in the first place if tracking wasn't set up.

**Upshot for our current decision:**
- `jj bookmark track main@origin -R .` is safe, correct, and one-time (won't need to re-run on future pushes).
- Worth filing a vc-x1 issue to copy the finalize check into push's preflight — catching this at preflight (before commits happen) is cleaner than catching it at push-app (after commits are locked in locally). This would have saved us from landing in this half-applied state right now.
- Also worth asking: should `vc-x1 init` (or `vc-x1 sync` on first run) auto-track? Probably yes — same reasoning. But out of scope for the current fix.

Still want me to proceed with the plan I outlined (gitignore + CLAUDE.md note → squash → `jj bookmark track` → resume push)?

> AGENT

Agreed — matches the "Late changes after push" pattern in CLAUDE.md. Current state: `D .vc-x1/push-state.toml` is pending in `@` (push's finalize-claude stage cleaned it up, producing a deletion we can use).

**Proposed commands** (3 steps, in order):

1. Squash the deletion into the pushed commit (needs `--ignore-immutable` since `10c22009` is already at origin):
   ```
   jj squash --ignore-immutable -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1
   ```
   This rewrites `10c22009` → new commit without `push-state.toml`. Working copy `@` becomes empty again.

2. Ensure `main` points to the rewritten commit:
   ```
   jj bookmark set main -r @- -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1
   ```
   (jj usually moves bookmarks automatically on rewrite, but explicit is safe.)

3. Push the rewritten commit — this **force-pushes** (rewrites remote history for `main`):
   ```
   jj git push --bookmark main -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1
   ```

**Note on risk** — step 3 rewrites the published `main` history. Since this push just happened ~a minute ago and no other collaborator has pulled, the blast radius is zero. But it is a force-push on a shared branch in principle.

`.claude` doesn't need this treatment — `push-state.toml` was only tracked in actor-x1's commit.

OK to run all three?

```
jj squash --ignore-immutable -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1
jj bookmark set main -r @- -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1
jj git push --bookmark main -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1
```

- First line folds the pending deletion (`D .vc-x1/push-state.toml`) into the already-pushed commit `10c22009` — produces a new commit with that file gone.
- Second line is a safety redundancy so `main` points at the rewritten commit.
- Third line force-pushes `main` to `origin`, rewriting the published history. Blast radius is zero (push finished ~a minute ago, nobody else has pulled), but it is a rewrite.

`.claude` doesn't need this.

> DEVELOPER

LG, before the push we see that squash's indicates with `main*` that it's tracked and changed and ready to be pushed, correct?

> AGENT

Yes — `main*` means the local bookmark differs from its tracked remote (`main@origin`). Specifically:
- No `*` → local and remote are in sync
- `*` → local has moved and is ahead/behind/diverged vs the remote

It requires tracking first (otherwise jj wouldn't even compare). So `main*` is exactly the signal "tracked, out of sync with origin, ready for `jj git push` to reconcile." In our case the `jj bookmark list --tracked` output will further show "ahead by 1, behind by 1" (the divergence from the rewrite), which `jj git push` handles as a force-push of the new commit.

> AGENT

The single-source-of-truth goal is right, but the current `CLAUDE.md` explicitly argues against putting durable bot context outside the repo (the Memory section: "hidden under the user's home, tied to one machine, invisible to anyone but the bot, never diffed or reviewed"). Moving the canonical workflow doc to `~/.claude/ws-dual-repo.md` reintroduces exactly those drawbacks: a new collaborator cloning the repo gets a broken import; the file isn't reviewable in PRs; version skew between projects becomes untraceable via any one repo's log.

A committed-per-project alternative that still gives you single-edit updates: keep the canonical file in `vc-template-x1` (it's already the source the user copies from), and add a `vc-x1 claude-sync` subcommand (or similar) that copies template → project when invoked. Preserves "committed, reviewable, visible" while eliminating manual duplication. One sibling todo for vc-x1's list, not a runtime import.

Also worth verifying: the file content is `@!/.claude/ws-dual-repo.x.md`, not `@~/.claude/...`. The `!` vs `~` looks like a typo (keyboard-adjacent on some layouts) — and `@!` isn't a Claude Code import syntax I recognize, though `@~` with tilde expansion might be. If Claude Code actually honors `@!`, that's news to me; if not, the local CLAUDE.md currently provides **no** instructions.

# CLAUDE.md - Bot Instructions

## Project Structure

This project uses **two separate jj-git repos**:

1. **App repo** (`/` — project root): Contains the application source code.
2. **Bot session repo** (`/.claude/`): Contains Claude Code session data.

Both repos are managed with `jj` (Jujutsu), which coexists with git.

## Repo Paths (relative from project root)

- App repo: `.` (project root)
- Bot session repo: `.claude`
  (symlink from `~/.claude/projects/<path-to-project-root>/.claude`)

The bot session repo is this project's **`other-repo`** — the
path is set by `[workspace].other-repo` in `.vc-config.toml`
(default `.claude`, as used here). Concrete examples below use
`.claude` for readability; treat the path as configurable where
it appears in prose.

## Working Directory

Prefer staying in the project root. Use `-R` flags or absolute paths
to target other directories rather than `cd`. If `cd` seems necessary,
discuss with the user first — losing track of cwd causes subtle
command failures downstream.

```
Based on the official Claude Code documentation on CLAUDE.md, here's what happens when an imported target file doesn't exist:

**The documentation does not specify the behavior for missing imports.** The relevant section on [importing additional files](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-md.md#import-additional-files) states:

> "CLAUDE.md files can import additional files using `@path/to/import` syntax... Imported files are expanded and loaded into context at launch alongside the CLAUDE.md that references them."

It mentions that:
- A dialog appears the **first time** Claude Code encounters external imports, listing the files and asking for approval
- If you decline, "the imports stay disabled and the dialog does not appear again"

However, it does **not** document what happens if the target file is missing—whether Claude silently continues, emits a warning, or errors out.

**Recommendation:** This is a gap in the documentation. Given your use case (relying on imports for distributed instructions), you should test this or raise the question via `/feedback` to get official clarification. A silent failure would indeed be dangerous for your workflow, and it's worth confirming the behavior before depending on it.

> AGENT

The guide agent couldn't find official documentation on missing-import behavior — Claude Code's docs state imports are "expanded and loaded into context at launch" but don't specify what happens when the target is missing (warning, silent drop, error, or something else). The agent recommended testing empirically or using `/feedback` to get clarification.

**Options to derisk without waiting on docs:**

- **Empirical test**: rename `~/.claude/ws-dual-repo.md` to `.bak`, start a fresh session, see whether Claude Code complains or loads an effectively-empty CLAUDE.md. One-time check; tells you the actual behavior.
- **Fallback content**: keep a minimal stub in the project-level CLAUDE.md alongside the import line — something like "If you see nothing after this, `~/.claude/ws-dual-repo.md` failed to import; check your setup." If Claude Code silently drops the import, the stub still shows. If it warns, no harm.
- **Switch patterns**: the earlier-discussed canonical-in-`vc-template-x1` + `vc-x1 claude-sync` approach sidesteps this entirely — the content is always physically present in the project.

The bot thinks the fallback-content option is cheapest: one extra line in each project's CLAUDE.md, defense in depth, no new tooling.

> AGENT

Expected — `*` tracks bookmark-vs-remote divergence, not working-copy status. Your `main` and `main@origin` both point to `c67a25df` (last pushed commit), so they're in sync → no `*`.

Your unpushed changes live in `@` (the mutable working-copy commit on top of `main`), not on `main` itself. Only a `jj commit` (or explicit `jj bookmark set`) would move `main` forward and produce the `*`. For "do I have pending changes?" use `jj st`; for "is my bookmark out of sync with origin?" use the `*` / `jj bookmark list --tracked`.
