# CLAUDE.md - Bot Instructions

## Project Structure

This project uses **two separate jj-git repos**:

1. **App repo** (`/` — project root): Contains the application source code.
2. **Bot session repo** (`/.claude/`): Contains Claude Code session data.

Both repos are managed with `jj` (Jujutsu), which coexists with git.

## Repo Paths (relative from project root)

- App repo: `.` (project root)
- Bot session repo: `.claude`
  (symlink from `~/.claude/projects/<path-to-project-root>/.claude`)

The bot session repo is this project's **`other-repo`** — the
path is set by `[workspace].other-repo` in `.vc-config.toml`
(default `.claude`, as used here). Concrete examples below use
`.claude` for readability; treat the path as configurable where
it appears in prose.

## Working Directory

Prefer staying in the project root. Use `-R` flags or absolute paths
to target other directories rather than `cd`. If `cd` seems necessary,
discuss with the user first — losing track of cwd causes subtle
command failures downstream.

## Memory

Do not use the bot's per-project memory directory
(`~/.claude/projects/<path>/memory/`). In a dual-repo setup with
CLAUDE.md it provides no capability CLAUDE.md doesn't already cover,
and it loses on discoverability:

- **CLAUDE.md** — at the repo root, a well-known location for bot
  instructions, committed, reviewable, visible to every collaborator
  (human or bot).
- **Memory directory** — hidden under the user's home, tied to one
  machine, invisible to anyone but the bot, never diffed or reviewed.

Easy for everyone to find beats convenient for the bot alone. Put
durable context in CLAUDE.md (or committed `notes/`) instead.

## Speculation marker

Durable text the bot writes — CLAUDE.md, `notes/`, commit bodies,
chores sections — should stick to observations and direct descriptions
of the code or data. If a mechanism, hypothesis, or causal claim
enters the text, prefix it with "The bot thinks ..." so a reader can
tell the measured from the inferred.

**Why:** unmarked speculation reads like evidence, and a future reader
(or the bot on a later session) can pick it up as a known fact when
it's not. Measured / inferred is a distinction worth keeping visible
in the written record.

**How to apply:** observations and factual descriptions need no
marker. Prefix with "The bot thinks ..." (or a close variant like
"The bot's guess is ...") when the claim is a mechanism ("X wins
because Y caches better"), a cause ("the drift was due to thermal
state"), a prediction ("this should scale linearly"), or any
reasoning not directly supported by the data on hand.

## Committing

Use `-R` (`--repository`) at the end to target the correct repo. Use
relative paths to reduce noise. Putting `-R` last keeps the verb/action
visible at the start of the command.

### App repo
```
jj commit -m \
"title" \
-m "body

ochid: /.claude/<changeID>" \
-R .
```

### Bot session repo
```
jj commit -m \
"title" \
-m "body

ochid: /<changeID>" \
-R .claude
```

## jj Basics

- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
  session because session data updates continuously.

## Commit Message Style

Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
a version suffix:

```
<type>: <short description> (<version>)
```

- **Title**: target ~50 chars, short summary of *what* changed.
  Include the version. Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`,
  `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
- **Body** (same across both repos): short intro paragraph (1–3
  sentences), then a terse bullet list. Each bullet corresponds
  one-to-one with the edits structure already documented in
  `notes/chores-*.md` for this step — just the file and a
  one-line gist (e.g. `README.md: new Overview intro`). Do *not*
  restate the detail that lives in chores; the commit body is a
  scan-able index, not a duplicate. The chores section is the
  source of truth.
- **Session notes** (optional tail, appended to the body): if the
  session produced items worth preserving that don't fit the
  code-change index — in-session tooling work, dead ends,
  rationale — append a trailing subsection titled
  `## <other-repo> session notes:` (e.g.
  `## .claude session notes:`) with those items as bullets. The
  same body (main content plus any session-notes subsection)
  lands in both commits.
- **Ochid trailer**: do *not* include `ochid:` in the body you
  pass via `--body`. Push appends the correct per-repo trailer
  at the commit stages (`ochid: /.claude/<chid>` for the app-repo
  commit; `ochid: /<chid>` for the `.claude` commit).
- Examples:
  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`
  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`
  - `refactor: deduplicate common CLI flags (0.21.1)`

## Pre-commit Requirements

### User approval

Never execute commit, squash, push, or finalize commands without the
user's explicit approval. Present changes for review first; only run
them after the user confirms. This applies to late changes too —
pause for review before squashing into an existing commit.

### Review before proposing the commit block

After finishing a unit of work, **summarize what changed and stop
there**. Do not pre-emptively lay out the Checkpoint-1 commit
commands. Wait for the user to signal review is complete before
proposing the commit block. Changes during review are the norm,
not the exception; proposing commit text too early creates noise
and signals that I consider the work done when it usually isn't.

This applies per-step in a multi-step flow too — each step gets a
review pause before its commit block appears.

Signals that review is complete include explicit approval ("let's
commit", "looks good, commit it") **and any directive to start the
next step** ("do step 4", "next", "go N+1"). In that case the
previous step must be committed first — always commit the current
step before starting the next; don't ask.

### Notes references

Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.

### Versioning

Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based
on scope:

- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump
  directly to `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-0`,
  implement across multiple commits incrementing the numeric
  suffix. The final commit drops the suffix.

The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before
starting.

For multi-step:

1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-0` with the plan and commit as a chore
   marker.
2. Implement in one or more `X.Y.Z-N` commits (increment N as
   needed).
3. Final commit bumps to `X.Y.Z` (no suffix), updates
   `notes/todo.md` and `notes/chores-*.md` — this is the "done"
   marker.

**Why numeric suffixes (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:**
semver pre-release identifiers may consist of a single numeric
component, and they compare numerically per spec. So
`X.Y.Z-1 < X.Y.Z-2 < … < X.Y.Z` correctly orders the dev ladder
below the done marker. Cargo accepts this form. The `-dev` prefix
adds no information the git log doesn't already convey and
doubles typing per commit.

The final release commit (no suffix) signals completion rather than
amending prior commits. This keeps history readable and makes it easy
to see which commits were exploratory vs final.

### Chores section headers

Chores section headers use trailing version format:

```
## Description (X.Y.Z)
```

Example: `` ## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0) ``

### Pre-commit checklist

Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:

1. `cargo fmt`
2. `cargo clippy`
3. `cargo test`
4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
5. Retest after install
6. Update `notes/todo.md` — add to `## Done` if completing a task
7. Update `notes/chores-*.md` — add a subsection describing the change
8. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new flags,
   new subcommands, changed behavior)

Push's preflight re-runs `vc-x1 sync` + `cargo fmt` +
`cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` + `cargo test` as a
safety net. Run the checklist above locally anyway — fmt / clippy /
test iteration is cheaper in-conversation than bouncing off
preflight after review approval. Steps 4–5 (`cargo install --path .`
and retest) are **not** in push's preflight (project-specific); the
bot runs them.

## Code Conventions

### Doc comments on every file, function, and method

Every `.rs` file must begin with a `//!` module docstring. Every
function and method must have a `///` doc comment. Keep them brief —
one sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the
comment exists, not that it be long.

This is a deliberate override of the generic "write no comments"
default that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the
module / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments
inside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a
non-obvious WHY.

### `// OK: …` comments on `unwrap*` calls (Rust)

Non-test code that calls `.unwrap()`, `.unwrap_or(…)`,
`.unwrap_or_default()`, or `.unwrap_or_else(…)` must have a trailing
`// OK: …` comment that justifies why the call is acceptable.

- `// OK: <specific reason>` — document the real precondition,
  invariant, or domain reason. Preferred whenever the reason isn't
  self-evident.
- `// OK: obvious` — the default is self-evident from context (e.g.
  `desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("")` — empty desc → empty title).

Bare `// OK` is not used (reads like a truncated comment).
Abbreviations (e.g. `SE`) are not used because they require a decoder
ring for readers seeing the code out of context.

For provably-unreachable `.unwrap()` calls, also prefix with
`#[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]` so the site stays silent if we enable
the project-wide `clippy::unwrap_used` lint later.

```rust
let max = stderr_level.unwrap_or(LevelFilter::Info); // OK: default verbosity when -v/-vv absent
let first_line = desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("");  // OK: obvious

match matches.len() {
    1 => {
        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
        // OK: `1 =>` arm guarantees matches.len() == 1
        Ok(TitleMatch::One(matches.into_iter().next().unwrap()))
    }
    // ...
}
```

Tests (`#[cfg(test)]`) are exempt — panicking on setup failure is the
correct test behavior.

### Ask for clarification on ambiguous input

When user input is ambiguous or missing necessary detail, stop and
ask a specific question. Do not proceed on a guess and hope the
result lands right — a clarifying question costs a few seconds;
redoing misaligned work costs much more.

### Recognize when stuck

If a simple task has eaten 5+ minutes of thinking or back-and-forth
without progress, stop. Summarize what's blocking — unclear
requirements, unfamiliar API surface, conflicting signals — and ask.
Continued flailing produces worse outcomes than a direct "I'm stuck
on X."

## ochid Trailers

Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
path followed by the changeID:

- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`

Use `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` to get both changeIDs (first line
is app repo, second is `.claude`).

## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow

**Prerequisite — one-time per repo:**

- `.gitignore` must include `/.vc-x1` so push's in-progress
  state files (`.vc-x1/push-state.toml`) don't get committed.
  Push warns at preflight if the entry is missing.
- The target bookmark must have a tracking remote
  (`jj bookmark track <bookmark>@origin -R .`). `jj git fetch`
  creates remote bookmarks as non-tracking by default; push
  currently surfaces this as a raw jj error at `push-app` after
  commits are locally made, so confirm tracking is set up
  before the first push in a new clone.

**Use `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` — it wraps the full flow in one
command with two interactive approval gates.** Push runs
preflight (sync + fmt + clippy + test), prompts for review,
composes the commit message (via `--title`/`--body` or `$EDITOR`),
prompts for message approval, then commits both repos → advances
bookmarks → pushes app → finalizes `.claude`. Failures inside the
local mutation window roll both repos back via `jj op restore`;
after `push-app` succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
recovery is forward-only.

**Run this flow after every step** — not only at session end.
Single-step and multi-step changes are of equal importance: a
single-step change is one `push` invocation; a multi-step change
is one `push` per `X.Y.Z-N` commit plus one for the final release
commit. Each step gets its own commit, its own push, and its own
finalize — so dev markers land on the remote and in `.claude` as
they happen rather than being batched until the end.

### Run `vc-x1 push`

```
vc-x1 push main                                      # interactive (review + $EDITOR)
vc-x1 push main --title "..." --body "..."           # flags skip $EDITOR
vc-x1 push main --yes --title "..." --body "..."     # full non-interactive
vc-x1 push main --dry-run                            # preview (no side effects)
vc-x1 push main --from commit-app                    # resume from specific stage
vc-x1 push --status                                  # show saved state
vc-x1 push main --restart                            # clear saved state; start fresh
```

The two approval gates are surfaced by push itself:

1. **Review** — push prints `jj diff --stat` for both repos and
   prompts `[y/N]`. Approve = "the work is done right".
2. **Message** — push either uses `--title`/`--body` (non-editor
   path) or opens `$EDITOR` on a template. Approve = "the message
   reads right".

Both titles and bodies are the **same** across the two commits;
per-repo `ochid:` trailers are appended by push itself. See
`## Commit Message Style` for body structure.

For the full flag list and stage machine, see `vc-x1 push --help`
and `notes/chores-05.md > Add push subcommand (0.37.0)`.

### Bot communication during the flow

When applying the flow on the user's behalf, use plain prose at
each gate — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal", "Checkpoint N",
etc.):

1. **After completing the work** — summarize what changed
   (file-by-file or feature-by-feature, terse) and end with
   something like:

   > Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the
   > commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.

2. **After review approval** — present the proposed `$TITLE` and
   `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to run `vc-x1 push`.
   Do **not** spell out the full
   `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title "$TITLE" --body "$BODY"`
   invocation by default — it's mechanical and obvious from the
   title/body. Show it only on explicit request (verbose mode /
   debugging).

3. **After execution approval** — run the push command. `push`
   handles commit + bookmark + push + finalize internally;
   nothing should be output after the push command (finalize is
   detached and absolute-last; see "After finalize: stop and
   wait" below).

### Pre-step: `vc-x1 sync` (still useful)

Push's preflight runs `vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run` as its first step
so divergence is resolved before the build. Running sync manually
before you *start* editing is still cheap (one line when clean)
and surfaces remote changes earlier:

```
vc-x1 sync                            # dual-repo workspace
vc-x1 sync -R .                       # single-repo project
vc-x1 sync --quiet                    # silent; exit code signals result
```

Output shape:

- **Clean**: one line — `sync: N repos, all up-to-date`. Proceed.
- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`): per-repo fetch +
  state lines, then `dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply`.
  Inspect, then re-run with `--no-dry-run` (or resolve conflicts
  if rebase fails).
- **`--quiet`**: no output; exit code is the only signal.

### After finalize: stop and wait

After `vc-x1 finalize` is launched — **whether mid-session per-step
or at session end** — you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit
files, run tools, or emit any text (prose, recaps, acknowledgements),
until the user explicitly directs you to continue. Treat finalize as
a hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words (e.g. "next is ...")
must be said in the approval prompt *before* executing finalize; the
finalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the turn and nothing
follows it.

This holds even when the next step seems obvious (e.g. "next is
N+1" or "now I should bump the version and commit the release").
Wait. The user controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint
they may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a break at.
Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint and produces unwanted writes
between finalize and the next explicit instruction.

Exceptions to this rule may emerge later but are not authorized at
this stage. Until told otherwise, treat as absolute.

### Late changes after push

If the app repo needs a tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.
updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is immutable. Use
`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:

```
jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
```

(`.claude` is also mutable via this pattern when needed, though
push's `finalize-claude` stage normally handles trailing session
writes so you rarely hit this case there.)

### Manual finalize fallback

If push exited before `finalize-claude` (e.g. `--no-finalize`
was set, or a failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`),
run finalize by hand:

```
vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
```

**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no
tool calls, no additional output. If any work is done after
finalize, run finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
already displays it.
---
-rw-r--r-- 1 wink users 18873 Apr 22 09:06 /home/wink/.claude/ws-dual-repo.ok.md
```

Found the Versioning section. Proposed edits (three places where "final = done, earlier = exploratory" framing needs softening):

**Edit A** — the Multi-step bullet at the top of Versioning:

From:
```
- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-0`,
  implement across multiple commits incrementing the numeric
  suffix. The final commit drops the suffix.
```

To:
```
- **Multi-step** (for scope that splits naturally into
  independently-reviewable steps): bump to `X.Y.Z-0`, implement
  across multiple `X.Y.Z-N` commits incrementing the numeric
  suffix. The final commit drops the suffix to close the ladder.
```

**Edit B** — steps 2–3 of "For multi-step":

From:
```
2. Implement in one or more `X.Y.Z-N` commits (increment N as
   needed).
3. Final commit bumps to `X.Y.Z` (no suffix), updates
   `notes/todo.md` and `notes/chores-*.md` — this is the "done"
   marker.
```

To:
```
2. Implement in one or more `X.Y.Z-N` commits (increment N as
   needed). Every `X.Y.Z-N` commit is a complete checkpoint in
   its own right — the `-N` suffix only signals that more steps
   in the same ladder are coming, not that the commit is partial
   or exploratory. Run the full pre-commit checklist (including
   `notes/todo.md` and `notes/chores-*.md` updates) on every
   `-N` commit exactly as you would on a single-step release.
3. Final commit bumps to `X.Y.Z` (no suffix) to close the ladder.
```

**Edit C** — tail paragraphs:

From:
```
**Why numeric suffixes (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:**
semver pre-release identifiers may consist of a single numeric
component, and they compare numerically per spec. So
`X.Y.Z-1 < X.Y.Z-2 < … < X.Y.Z` correctly orders the dev ladder
below the done marker. Cargo accepts this form. The `-dev` prefix
adds no information the git log doesn't already convey and
doubles typing per commit.

The final release commit (no suffix) signals completion rather than
amending prior commits. This keeps history readable and makes it easy
to see which commits were exploratory vs final.
```

To:
```
**Why numeric suffixes (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:**
semver pre-release identifiers may consist of a single numeric
component, and they compare numerically per spec. So
`X.Y.Z-1 < X.Y.Z-2 < … < X.Y.Z` correctly orders the ladder:
each `-N` sorts before its final unsuffixed sibling. Cargo
accepts this form. The `-dev` prefix adds no information the
git log doesn't already convey and doubles typing per commit.

The final release commit (no suffix) closes the ladder rather
than amending prior commits. This keeps history readable: a
chain of complete checkpoints ending in an `X.Y.Z` marker.
```

> AGENT

**On updating `.claude`**: the app working copy is empty after the squash (`@` is `vyvtwkyv 1e67a65f (empty)`), so running `vc-x1 push` would produce an empty app commit. Cleaner is a **standalone finalize** on `.claude` — same command push's `finalize-claude` stage uses, and exactly the "Manual finalize fallback" case in CLAUDE.md:

```
vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push main --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
```

That captures trailing session writes without touching the app repo.

**On the push feature idea**: yes, worth filing. The pattern we just did twice (edit → `jj squash --ignore-immutable` → `jj git push`) is the "amend and force-push" workflow, which push currently delegates to manual jj commands. An opt-in `--amend` flag on push would:

- Detect "working-copy change sits on top of the latest pushed commit"
- Squash the working copy into `@-`
- Advance bookmarks, force-push app, re-finalize `.claude`
- Optionally accept `--title`/`--body` to update the commit message too (pure content-only amend if omitted)

The bot thinks **opt-in** is the right default: force-push is destructive, collaborators' pulls get clobbered silently, and "amend an already-pushed commit" should always be a conscious choice. Auto-detecting and prompting would be a middle ground.

Only app-repo amend vs both-repos amend is another design choice — most late changes are docs/chores affecting only the app repo; `.claude` tends to just accumulate session data. Could default to app-only and let `--amend both` extend to both.

File as a vc-x1 todo, or do you want to sketch the design further first?

1	//! actor-x1 — an experiment in the actor model in Rust.
2	//!
3	//! See `notes/design.md` for the staged design. Stage 1 provides
4	//! a minimal runtime with two actors that ping-pong an empty
5	//! [`Message`] for a caller-supplied duration; Goal1 runs both
6	//! actors on one thread, Goal2 runs each actor on its own thread.
7	//!
8	//! Public surface today:
9	//! - [`Message`]: the empty unit message Stage 1 actors exchange.
10	//! - [`Actor`]: the single-method actor trait.
11	//! - [`Context`]: the send-side handle passed to each dispatch.
12	//! - [`runtime`]: concrete runtimes (single-thread for Goal1; the
13	//!   multi-thread runtime will land in 0.1.0-3).
14	
15	pub mod perf;
16	pub mod runtime;
17	
18	/// Empty unit-type message exchanged between Stage 1 actors.
19	/// Carries no payload by design — Stage 1's contract is only
20	/// "dispatch happens"; Stage 2 replaces this with a richer type.
21	pub struct Message;
22	
23	/// Actor behavior: handle one [`Message`] at a time, never blocking.
24	///
25	/// The only method is [`Actor::handle_message`]; all outbound
26	/// communication goes through the [`Context`] the runtime supplies.
27	pub trait Actor {
28	    /// Handle a single incoming message. Use `ctx` to emit any
29	    /// resulting sends. Must not block.
30	    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, msg: Message);
31	}
32	
33	/// Runtime-supplied send handle passed to each
34	/// [`Actor::handle_message`] call. Implementations differ between
35	/// runtimes: the single-thread runtime writes to a shared queue;
36	/// the (future) multi-thread runtime writes to per-actor channels.
37	pub trait Context {
38	    /// Enqueue `msg` for delivery to the actor identified by
39	    /// `dst_id`. Returns immediately; delivery is the runtime's job.
40	    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message);
41	}
42	
```

  "content": "//! `tprobe` — tick-counting performance probe for short code-path\n//! measurement.\n//!\n//! Vendored from\n//! Graduated from\n//! `actor-x1`'s in-tree `src/perf/` module at `actor-x1 0.2.0-2`.\n//!\n//! Files:\n//! - [`tprobe2`] — scope-based probe (`start` / `end` → record buffer\n//!   → histogram at `report` time).\n//! - [`band_table`] — shared percentile-band renderer.\n//! - [`ticks`] — hardware tick counter (x86_64 `rdtsc`).\n//! - [`fmt`] — `fmt_commas` / `fmt_commas_f64` extracted from\n//!   `iiac-perf/src/harness.rs` so we don't carry the whole 393-line\n//!   benchmark harness for two helpers.\n//! - [`overhead`] — two-point apparatus-overhead calibration for\n//!   [`TProbe2`].\n//! - [`pin`] — CPU-affinity helpers (thread pinning).\n//!\n//! Divergences from upstream at the time of graduation:\n//! - Import paths reflect the standalone-crate layout (`crate::x`\n//!   rather than `crate::perf::x`).\n//! - `// OK: …` justifications on every `unwrap*` call per the\n//!   host workspace's code conventions.\n//! - `ticks.rs` diagnostic string mentions \"tprobe\" rather than\n//!   \"iiac-perf\".\n\npub(crate) mod band_table;\npub(crate) mod fmt;\npub mod overhead;\npub mod pin;\npub mod ticks;\npub mod tprobe2;\n\npub use overhead::{Overhead, calibrate};\npub use tprobe2::{TProbe2, TProbe2RecId};\n"
}

1	//! Shared band-table renderer for tick-valued histograms.
2	//!
3	//! Both `TProbe` (fast path, direct-histogram) and `TProbe2`
4	//! (scope API, records → drain) store hardware tick deltas and
5	//! want the same band-table output shape — min/p1/…/p99/max
6	//! rows with first/last/range/count/mean columns, plus summary
7	//! lines for mean, stdev, mean min-p99, stdev min-p99. This
8	//! module provides a single implementation both can call into.
9	//!
10	//! Display unit is chosen by `as_ticks`: `false` converts stored

592	### Design decisions recorded here
593	
594	- **Virtual workspace, `crates/` subdir** (option (b) in the
595	  plan discussion): root is `[workspace]`-only, both packages
596	  live under `crates/`. Cleaner symmetric layout than mixing
597	  `[package]` and `[workspace]` at the root, at the cost of a
598	  one-time move of every actor-x1 source file.
599	- **Crate name `tprobe` drops the "2"**. The type rename is
600	  deferred to its own step (`0.2.0-3`) to keep the move diff
601	  (`0.2.0-2`) separate from the rename diff — easier review
602	  for each.
603	- **Perf deps move with the perf code.** `hdrhistogram`,
604	  `minstant`, and `core_affinity` migrate out of actor-x1's
605	  `Cargo.toml` in `0.2.0-2`; actor-x1 picks them up transitively
606	  via `tprobe` without redeclaring.
607

  "old_string": "- `cargo install --path crates/actor-x1` replaces the prior\n  `actor-x1 v0.2.0-0` install; `goal1 --version` and\n  `goal2 --version` both report `actor-x1 0.2.0-1`.",
  "new_string": "- `cargo install --path crates/actor-x1` replaces the prior\n  `actor-x1 v0.2.0-0` install; `goal1 --version` and\n  `goal2 --version` both report `actor-x1 0.2.0-1`.\n\n## Move perf into tprobe crate (0.2.0-2)\n\nCarries out the body of the `0.2.0` ladder: `src/perf/*` moves\nfrom `crates/actor-x1/src/` into `crates/tprobe/src/`; perf\ndependencies migrate with the code; actor-x1 picks `tprobe` up\nas a path dependency. Behavior unchanged — tests and binaries\nrun the same workloads with identical output modulo the version\nbanner. The `TProbe2 → TProbe` rename is deliberately deferred\nto `0.2.0-3`.\n\n- `Cargo.lock`: updated by cargo for the new dep graph.\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: version `0.2.0-1` → `0.2.0-2`;\n  drops `core_affinity`, `hdrhistogram`, `minstant`; adds\n  `tprobe = { path = \"../tprobe\" }`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: drops `pub mod perf;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime.rs`: `use crate::perf::TProbe2`\n  → `use tprobe::TProbe2`; `crate::perf::pin::pin_current(...)`\n  → `tprobe::pin::pin_current(...)`. `cargo fmt` reordered the\n  import block (local `crate::` before external `tprobe`) per\n  rustfmt's group-ordering.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`: `use actor_x1::perf::{self, pin, ticks}`\n  → `use tprobe::{self as perf, pin, ticks}`. Call sites (e.g.\n  `perf::calibrate()`) unchanged — the `self as perf` alias\n  preserves them.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`: same import rewrite as\n  goal1.\n- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: version bumped `0.1.0-0` → `0.1.0`\n  (skeleton → first functional release); adds `core_affinity`,\n  `hdrhistogram`, `minstant` to `[dependencies]`.\n- `crates/tprobe/src/lib.rs`: rewritten from docstring-only stub\n  to the full module root — merges the vendored-from-iiac-perf\n  notes previously in `src/perf/mod.rs`, declares the six\n  submodules (`band_table`, `fmt`, `overhead`, `pin`, `ticks`,\n  `tprobe2`), and re-exports `Overhead` / `calibrate` /\n  `TProbe2` / `TProbe2RecId` at the crate root.\n- `crates/tprobe/src/band_table.rs`, `fmt.rs`, `overhead.rs`,\n  `pin.rs`, `ticks.rs`, `ticks/x86_64.rs`, `tprobe2.rs`: moved\n  verbatim from `crates/actor-x1/src/perf/`. Internal imports\n  rewritten — every `crate::perf::x` (both `use` lines and\n  doc-link references) becomes `crate::x` since within `tprobe`\n  the crate root *is* what `perf/mod.rs` used to be.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- **`tprobe` version bumps `0.1.0-0` → `0.1.0`** at this step\n  rather than mirroring actor-x1's `-N` ladder. tprobe's own\n  history is \"skeleton → populated\", which is a single one-step\n  release in its own right. Drawing its version clock separately\n  from actor-x1's matches the \"each crate versions itself\"\n  expectation callers have of any Cargo dep.\n- **`use tprobe::{self as perf, pin, ticks}`** in the binaries\n  preserves `perf::calibrate()` at the call site. Could have\n  been rewritten to `tprobe::calibrate()` — chose the alias to\n  keep the move diff (this step) minimal so `0.2.0-3`'s rename\n  sweep is the only place the identifier changes. The alias\n  disappears when the bot thinks the call site should read\n  `tprobe::…` directly, which is a follow-up worth considering\n  after the rename lands.\n- **Imports inside tprobe drop the `perf::` segment** rather\n  than keeping a nominal `perf` submodule at `crate::perf`. A\n  standalone crate's root *is* the top of the namespace; adding\n  an intermediate `perf` module inside `tprobe` would be\n  redundant and would force every call site to say\n  `tprobe::perf::x` — worse in every direction.\n\n### Verification\n\n- `cargo fmt` clean after one small auto-reorg in\n  `runtime.rs` (see bullet above).\n- `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean across both\n  members.\n- `cargo test` — 22 pass (5 in actor-x1 runtime tests + 17 in\n  tprobe's moved unit tests) = 22/22. Same count as before the\n  move, confirming no test was lost.\n- `cargo install --path crates/actor-x1` replaces the prior\n  `actor-x1 v0.2.0-1` install; `goal1 --version` and\n  `goal2 --version` both report `actor-x1 0.2.0-2`."
}

**Body**:
```
Carries out the body of the 0.2.0 ladder: `src/perf/*` graduates
from `actor-x1` into `tprobe`. actor-x1 picks up `tprobe` as a
path dependency; perf deps (`core_affinity`, `hdrhistogram`,
`minstant`) migrate with the code. Behavior unchanged — same 22
tests, same binaries, same reports. `TProbe2 → TProbe` rename
deferred to 0.2.0-3.

- Cargo.lock: updated by cargo for the new dep graph.
- crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml: 0.2.0-1 → 0.2.0-2; drops
  `core_affinity`/`hdrhistogram`/`minstant`; adds
  `tprobe = { path = "../tprobe" }`.
- crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs: drops `pub mod perf;`.
- crates/actor-x1/src/runtime.rs: `crate::perf::TProbe2` →
  `tprobe::TProbe2`; `crate::perf::pin::pin_current` →
  `tprobe::pin::pin_current`. `cargo fmt` reordered the import
  block.
- crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs: `actor_x1::perf::{self,
  pin, ticks}` → `tprobe::{self as perf, pin, ticks}` (alias
  preserves `perf::calibrate()` call site).
- crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs: same import rewrite.
- crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml: 0.1.0-0 → 0.1.0; adds
  `core_affinity`/`hdrhistogram`/`minstant`.
- crates/tprobe/src/lib.rs: stub replaced with full module root
  (vendoring notes + `mod`/`pub use` declarations).
- crates/tprobe/src/{band_table, fmt, overhead, pin, ticks,
  ticks/x86_64, tprobe2}.rs: moved verbatim from
  `crates/actor-x1/src/perf/`; internal `crate::perf::x` →
  `crate::x` (`use` lines and doc-link references).
- notes/chores-01.md: new `## Move perf into tprobe crate
  (0.2.0-2)` section.
- notes/todo.md: `Move perf into tprobe crate (0.2.0-2) [[5]]`
  added to `## Done` + reference.
```

1	//! Shared band-table renderer for tick-valued histograms.
2	//!
3	//! Both `TProbe` (fast path, direct-histogram) and `TProbe2`
4	//! (scope API, records → drain) store hardware tick deltas and
5	//! want the same band-table output shape — min/p1/…/p99/max
6	//! rows with first/last/range/count/mean columns, plus summary
7	//! lines for mean, stdev, mean min-p99, stdev min-p99. This
8	//! module provides a single implementation both can call into.
9	//!
10	//! Display unit is chosen by `as_ticks`: `false` converts stored
11	//! tick values to nanoseconds via [`crate::ticks::ticks_per_ns`];
12	//! `true` shows raw ticks.

21	];

23	    "min", "p1", "p10", "p20", "p30", "p40", "p50", "p60", "p70", "p80", "p90", "p99", "max",
24	];

The \"2\" only carried\nmeaning inside upstream `iiac-perf`, where it distinguished\nthis scope-API probe from a sibling direct-histogram `TProbe`.\nInside this standalone crate there's only one probe, so the\nsuffix is noise.\n\n- `Cargo.lock`: updated by cargo for the tprobe version bump.\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.2.0-2` → `0.2.0-3`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime.rs`: six textual occurrences of\n  `TProbe2` → `TProbe` (field type, `use`, constructor call,\n  `probe_mut` return type, and two doc-links).\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`: docstring phrase\n  `` `tprobe2` band-table report `` → `` `tprobe` band-table\n  report ``.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`: two copies of the same\n  docstring phrase updated.\n- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: `0.1.0` → `0.1.1` (patch bump —\n  public API rename).\n- `crates/tprobe/src/lib.rs`: module declaration\n  `pub mod tprobe2` → `pub mod tprobe`; re-export\n  `pub use tprobe2::{TProbe2, TProbe2RecId}` → `pub use\n  tprobe::{TProbe, TProbeRecId}`; two docstring references\n  (`[`tprobe2`]` → `[`tprobe`]`, `[`TProbe2`]` → `[`TProbe`]`).\n- `crates/tprobe/src/tprobe.rs` (was `tprobe2.rs`): file\n  renamed; all `TProbe2` / `TProbe2RecId` identifier\n  occurrences → `TProbe` / `TProbeRecId` (struct defs, impls,\n  tests, doc-links); band-table header string `\"tprobe2\"` →\n  `\"tprobe\"`.\n- `crates/tprobe/src/overhead.rs`: four doc-link references\n  `TProbe2` / `TProbe2::report` → `TProbe` / `TProbe::report`.\n- `crates/tprobe/src/band_table.rs`: module-level docstring\n  reworded — previously referenced both `TProbe` (fast path)\n  and `TProbe2` (scope API) from upstream; now notes that\n  only the scope-API probe was vendored and still shares the\n  renderer. Function docstring example list trimmed from\n  `(`\"tprobe\"`, `\"tprobe2\"`, …)` to `(e.g. `\"tprobe\"`)`.\n- `notes/chores-01.md`: this section. Earlier sections that\n  record the arrival of `TProbe2` during Stage 1 (`0.1.0-2`,\n  `0.1.0-3`, `0.1.0-6`) are left as-is — they describe events\n  at the time, when the identifier was literally `TProbe2`.\n- `notes/todo.md`: `Rename TProbe2 → TProbe (0.2.0-3) [[6]]`\n  added to `## Done` + reference.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- **Single `replace_all` on `TProbe2` handles both identifiers**\n  because `TProbe2RecId` contains `TProbe2` as a prefix; the\n  substring replacement produces `TProbeRecId` without a\n  separate pass.\n- **The submodule keeps the name `tprobe`**, matching the crate\n  name. External callers reach types via the `pub use` at the\n  crate root (`tprobe::TProbe`); the fully-qualified internal\n  path `tprobe::tprobe::TProbe` is slightly awkward but never\n  written by consumers. The alternative — moving the struct\n  directly into `lib.rs` and eliminating the submodule — would\n  inflate `lib.rs` with ~290 lines of probe implementation\n  and make the crate root less navigable. Keeping the\n  submodule is cheaper for readability.\n- **tprobe version bumps `0.1.0` → `0.1.1`** rather than\n  `0.2.0`. Pre-1.0 semver calls for a minor bump on API break,\n  but `actor-x1` is the only consumer, is updated atomically\n  in the same commit, and doesn't pin a version requirement\n  (path deps skip that). The bump is informational; a patch\n  level is enough to signal \"content changed\" in cargo's\n  accounting.\n\n### Verification\n\n- `cargo fmt` clean (no reorgs required this time).\n- `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean across both\n  members.\n- `cargo test` — 22 pass (5 in actor-x1 + 17 in tprobe). Same\n  count as `0.2.0-2`; rename is mechanical so no test delta\n  expected.\n- `cargo install --path crates/actor-x1` replaces the prior\n  `actor-x1 v0.2.0-2` install; both binaries report\n  `actor-x1 0.2.0-3`.\n- No remaining `TProbe2` or `tprobe2` (case-sensitive) in the\n  `crates/` tree per a final grep — confirms the sweep is\n  complete."

40	## Usage
41	
42	Stage 1 (the PoC landed at 0.1.0) provides two binaries:
43	
44	- **`goal1`** — two actors on one thread, ping-ponging a
45	  message through a shared `VecDeque`.
46	- **`goal2`** — two actors on two threads, ping-ponging over
47	  `std::sync::mpsc` channels.
48	
49	Install with `cargo install --path .`; both share a common CLI
50	shape:
51	
52	```
53	$ goal1 0.5 --warmup 0 --inner 1000 --pin 0
54	actor-x1 0.1.0
55	goal1: 102087000 messages in 0.500s (204.174 M msg/s, inner=1000)
56	  pinning: main → core 0
57	  apparatus: framing=21 tk (5.54 ns); per-event at inner=1000 = 0 tk (0.00 ns)
58	
59	  tprobe2: goal1.dispatch [count=102,087]
60	              first     last    range        count mean
61	    min-p1        0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
62	    p1-p10        0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
63	    p10-p20       4 ns     4 ns     0 ns    32,684    4 ns
64	    p20-p30       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
65	    p30-p40       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
66	    p40-p50       5 ns     5 ns     0 ns    30,128    5 ns
67	    p50-p60       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
68	    p60-p70       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
69	    p70-p80       5 ns     5 ns     0 ns    34,218    5 ns
70	    p80-p90       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
71	    p90-p99       5 ns     7 ns     2 ns     3,971    6 ns
72	    p99-max       7 ns    34 ns    27 ns     1,086    9 ns
73	    mean                                              5 ns
74	    stdev                                             1 ns
75	    mean min-p99                                      5 ns
76	    stdev min-p99                                     0 ns
77	```
78	
79	### Flags
80	
81	- **`<duration_s>`** — positional, required; measurement window
82	  in seconds.
83	- **`--warmup <SECS>`** — default 10.0; run the same dispatch
84	  loop with the probe active for this long, then clear the probe
85	  at the boundary so only steady-state data reaches the report.
86	  Set to 0 for quick iteration.
87	- **`--inner <N>`** *(goal1 only)* — default 1, ≥1; dispatches
88	  per probe scope. Larger values amortize the probe's two-`rdtsc`
89	  apparatus cost and give the histogram meaningful dynamic range.
90	  goal2 fixes `inner=1` because its per-channel queue holds at
91	  most one message.
92	- **`-t` / `--ticks`** — display probe values as raw ticks
93	  instead of nanoseconds.
94	- **`--raw`** — skip apparatus-overhead calibration; show the raw
95	  per-event cost including framing contamination.
96	- **`--pin <CORES>`** — pin threads to logical CPUs. goal1 uses
97	  the first core in the list; goal2 pins actor `i` to
98	  `cores[i % len]`. Accepts comma lists and ranges: `--pin 5`,
99	  `--pin 0,1`, `--pin 0-3`, `--pin 0,0` (oversubscribe).
100	
101	### Typical invocations
102	
103	```
104	# Short iterative smoke, no warmup:
105	goal1 0.1 --warmup 0 --inner 1000
106	
107	# Full measurement with pinning:
108	goal1 2 --inner 1000 --pin 0
109	
110	# Goal2 with actors on separate cores (same complex):
111	goal2 1 --pin 0,1
112	```
113	
114	## Reading the band table
115	
116	The `tprobe2` block is a 12-row percentile histogram with four
117	summary rows. Every row renders every run, even when a band
118	holds no data — a zero row is distinguishable from a missing row.
119	
120	### Header
121	
122	```
123	tprobe2: <name> [count=N]
124	```
125	
126	`<name>` identifies the probe (`goal1.dispatch`,
127	`goal2.dispatch.actor0`, …). `count=N` is the number of **scopes**
128	drained into the histogram, not total dispatches — at `--inner
129	1000` one scope covers 1,000 dispatches, so `count` here is
```
